# VSS Gaming-GPU — Execution Ledger

**Spec (source of truth):** [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-23-vss-gaming-gpu-profile-design.md`](../superpowers/specs/2026-09-23-vss-gaming-gpu-profile-design.md) (rev 5; the rev-4 cite here was stale until this row fixed it — see F10) · **Brief:** [`docs/vss-integration/13-implementation-brief.md`](../vss-integration/13-implementation-brief.md) · **Branch:** `feat/vss-gaming-gpu-profile` · **Phase plans:** [`G0`](../superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-vss-g0-gb300-environment.md) (CLOSED 2026-09-24) → [`P0`](../superpowers/plans/2026-09-24-vss-p0-foundations.md) (CLOSED 2026-09-25 — M0) → [`Phase 1`](../superpowers/plans/2026-09-25-vss-phase1-vlm-path.md) (current; one phase at a time)

One row per step (G0.1…4.3) and per spike (S-1…S-5). Statuses: `TODO` · `DOING` · `DONE` · `BLOCKED` (reason) · `NA`. A milestone row closes only with its S# numbers and a commit. Evidence carries the [V]/[C]/[E]/[?]/[O]/[A] markers per [`docs/vss-integration/AGENTS.md`](../vss-integration/AGENTS.md). A step closes on an executed command + result + commit, never on written code.

The model read every one correctly and confirmed the ONE genuine forced-entry photo at 95/100, zero FPs — the verifier is calibrated; **keyword stock search cannot build incident scenes, because nobody publishes real doorbell break-ins as encyclopedic media.** Committed kinetics/cosmos sets carry `source_path` to the owner's original generation machine (`/home/msvoboda/...`) — those frames, if they still exist off-repo, ARE the valid incident media for these labels. Owner decision F5-refined. Aggregate only; rows in `out/s3_stock/results.json`. **Re-run attempt + re-tally amendment 2026-09-25 [V]:** re-run BLOCKED — (a) the incident half stays F5-gated (owner synthetic generation unscheduled; `out/media/` holds only the stock set), (b) no live endpoint at attempt time and `agent-gpu run … llama-server` (the exact G0.2/F1 recipe) was denied twice by the sandbox permission system, so no serve was started (re-dispatch when the broker grants it). Re-tally of `out/s3_stock/results.json` (75 rows, same corpus — a re-run would add nothing): 75/75 `parse_ok`, **benign 27/27 verdict `uncertain`** → `correct` 0/27, incidents 46 `uncertain` + 2 `confirmed` → `correct` 2/48 (this row's prose "1/48" undercounts by one — the ledger's own aggregate block already says 2/48; re-counted from the JSON in-session). Reading, honestly: "0 false positives" remains TRUE (nothing benign was flagged) and the incident-half diagnosis stands unchanged, but on THIS stock corpus the strict bars (benign REJECTED, not merely un-flagged; ≥1/48 incidents confirmed) are not met — the conservative Qwen3-VL-4B at temp 0 chose `uncertain` on 73/75 frames. The genuine unblock is owner-generated born-labeled incident media (Task 0) plus an owner call whether `uncertain`-on-frames satisfies the bar. The placeholder run (10/10 schema-valid, 6/6 benign rejected) is untouched by this amendment [V] | —        |

## Counting erratum: the "61 risk_score sites" (step 0.25)

Spec §8 step 0.25 says **61 sites at `4eab98e5`**, found with `grep -rnE 'risk_score\s*(>=|<=|>|<)'` [V spec:386-387]. Not reproducible by any scope tried (re-run 2026-09-24; the grep set is byte-identical at `4eab98e5`, at `d3841e19`, and at G0-close HEAD, so the counts below are stable across those SHAs — an earlier "at HEAD d3841e19" label here was itself stale, `d3841e19` predated this ledger):

| Recipe                                                                              | Count                       |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------- |
| `git grep -nE 'risk_score\s*(>=\|<=\|>\|<)' -- backend/ ':(exclude)backend/tests/'` | **54** in 24 files          |
| same, whole `backend/` (incl. tests)                                                | 119                         |
| recipe A **+ arithmetic** (`risk_score\s*[*/+-]`, tests excluded)                   | 58 (54 + 4 arithmetic-only) |

Recipe A + arithmetic = **58** confirmed at G0 close (2026-09-24). The 4 arithmetic-only sites are real live-path code, not docstrings: `analytics.py:418` (histogram `risk_score / bucket_size`), `experiment_result.py:117` (`abs(v1 - v2)`), `calibration_service.py:407` (`risk_score / 50.0`), `pipeline_quality_audit_service.py:314` (`abs(audit.consistency_risk_score - event.risk_score)`) — all four TypeError on a NULL score, so all four belong to the 0.25 audit surface. (The `+ - * //` prose earlier in this row was an invalid ERE, `[+-]` alone parses as a range; the valid class is `[*/+-]`.)

**Working set for step 0.25: the 54-site recipe** (live comparisons, tests excluded) **+ the 4 arithmetic sites = 58**. The true code surface is smaller still: many of the 54 are docstrings/OpenAPI descriptions, not live code — a per-site triage belongs in the 0.25 plan, which will classify each as live-path / dead / comment. The "61" figure is superseded; the dated errata entry is **already written** — N1–N7 exist in `docs/vss-integration/11-errata-2026-09-23.md` (this row's earlier "not yet written" was stale; corrected at G0 close). Spec text stays untouched per the brief.

## P0.25 — null-safety: triage and shipped set (2026-09-25)

Triage of the 58-site surface (54 comparison + 4 arithmetic, recipe `[*/+-]`), adversarially re-checked (6-cluster workflow `wf_a178969e-6f0` + direct code reads adjudicating its exclusions): **5 of 8 named consumers were already NULL-safe** — alert*engine:451 (`is None or <` since the file's first commit), analytics:418 SQL (WHERE `risk_score is not null`), experiment_result:117 (columns `nullable=False`), calibration:407 (caller guard `adjust_from_feedback:250`, already pinned by its own test), audit:314 (`is not None and` guard, already pinned) — plus validators:191/198 (callers pass schema-validated ints), event_repository:156 (SQL WHERE excludes NULL), sampling:355 (`isinstance` guard), trend_service:116 (guarded `is not None` upstream), models/event.py:41-45 (`_compute_risk_level` guards None first — verified via workflow caller-trace: FastAPI returns `{"risk_score": null, "risk_level": null}` cleanly), the rest docstrings/OpenAPI prose/SQL expressions. **Correction to the counting erratum above: "all four arithmetic sites TypeError on NULL" was wrong** — direct reads + 4 green pin-tests (test_null_score_p025.py::TestArithmeticSitesAreAlreadyNullSafe_PINS) show each is NULL-safe by a different mechanism; the sites still belonged to the audit surface (they were checked; two already had pin tests) — the \_reason* was wrong, the _inclusion_ was right.

**Fixed (red-first, test_null_score_p025.py: 8 red → 16 green):** `WebSocketEventData.risk_score/risk_level` → Optional (spec §4; both WS contract tests updated to assert the new anyOf-with-bounded-scored-branch shape); `requires_ack` null-safe (`risk_score is not None and >= 80`; critical-level arm keeps ack; a None event no longer TypeErrors the subscriber loop); `notification_filter.should_notify` §6-step-3 detector-only rule **before** any level/threshold comparison (new `detection_class`/`detection_confidence` params; `SECURITY_RELEVANT_CLASSES = {person, vehicle}` exactly as spec names — widening is an owner call; NULL+strong→notify even past a 90 camera threshold, NULL+weak/non-security/no-evidence→suppress; global-disable still first). Frontend: `SecurityEventData.risk_score/risk_level` → `| null`; ActivityFeed draws an `Unverified` chip instead of a badge (design is 1.6's; this is no-crash/no-lie), DashboardPage gauge shows latest **known** score (never zero-for-a-lie), riskHistory drops unscored points, LiveActivitySection counts/surfaces nothing for unverified. `getRiskLevel` untouched — its throw-on-non-number is the no-lie guard.

Gates [V]: unit 27,988 passed (1 known systemctl env fail), contracts 612 (612 with updated pins), integration failure-set **byte-identical to clean-HEAD baseline** (Redis/shutdown flakes pre-existing; targeted HEAD runs prove the flake, diff of sets empty), vitest 1061/1061 + typecheck clean, `gen-ai-contract.py --check` current. Legacy diff = the approved set + nothing else: 3 backend files + 2 contract tests + 4 frontend files + 1 new test file (+110/−29 code, tests counted separately).

## P0 execution notes

- **P0.25 premise check — PASSED 2026-09-25 [V].** `backend/models/event.py:58-60`: `risk_score` and `risk_level` are both `Mapped[int | None]` / `Mapped[str | None]`, `nullable=True` — the plan's STOP-AND-ASK trip (column NOT NULL ⇒ 0.3's premise breaks) does not fire; NULL scores are storable today. The gap is purely the WebSocket schema (`websocket.py:252-253`: `risk_score: int = Field(...)`, `risk_level: RiskLevel` required) — exactly Task 2's edit target.

- **P0.1 control freeze — CODE-DONE / execution-ruled-N/A 2026-09-25 [V] (F9).** `backend/evaluation/control_freeze.py` ships tested: freeze = copy event images INTO the store dir (survives any later retention purge) + honest `AssessInput` snapshot (zones/household are NOT on event rows → empty, never invented) + control = the RECORDED score (`Event.risk_score`, falling back to parsing `LLMInteraction.raw_response`; no control at all → loud skip) + owner label via spec §5's table verbatim (`false_positive`→benign; `missed_threat`/`accurate`-on-high→incident with `expected_severity`, bare-accurate falling back to the event's own score band); provenance rides the item_id (`pre-switch::{event_id}`, no FK — the EvalItem contract has no linkage field and none was invented). Unlabeled events freeze with the EMPTY label (contract stays `str` as owner-reviewed; the frozen analogue of a NULL verdict; S2/S3 skip empties — a §5 item with no label still gets its control run, which is why unlabeled must not skip). Manifest = JSONL one row per item incl. loud-skip rows with reasons. D10: store + media-root residence prefight (repo/capture root = whole-run `ControlFreezeError`, not a skip), per-source containment guard (no copy from inside the media tree), `EvalStore.put_item` still decides item media at write time. Tier note: aiosqlite is NOT in this repo's dependency set — the plan's "sqlite or the test PG" fixture branch taken is the TEST PG: core unit-pinned on fabricated loaded rows (21 green), ORM seam live-PG-pinned (3 green, same-session compose, E11 honored). **Gate-tooling erratum, 2026-09-25 [V]:** the goal's commit-message recipe (`uv tool run pre-commit run conventional-pre-commit --hook-stage commit-msg --commit-msg-filename <file>`) FALSE-REJECTS any dotted scope — it mis-parses `feat(p0.25):`/`feat(p0.3):` and even rejects the already-accepted `b79bbb1f` message verbatim (proved by running the wrapper against it). The tool's own direct invocation validates both. Use `uv tool run conventional-pre-commit <file>` (optionally with the repo's allowed types `feat fix docs chore refactor test`) for dotted-scope messages; the wrapper stays fine for undotted scopes. **Amendment 2026-09-27 [V] — the types are SPACE-separated, and passing them comma-joined silently disables the check's allow-list.** `uv tool run conventional-pre-commit fix,feat,docs /tmp/msg.txt` makes the tool treat the whole string as ONE type, so nothing matches and only its built-in defaults (`feat`, `fix`, `perf`, `refactor`, `ci`, `build`, `test`) still pass: a `fix(...)` message validates and the check looks healthy, then the first `docs(...)` message rejects and reads like a message defect. Caught exactly that way — a real, valid `docs(vss)` message was rejected, diagnosed by bisecting to a header-only file, and confirmed against the repo's own hook args (`.pre-commit-config.yaml`, which lists the types one per line). The safe invocation is the no-types form, which reads the repo config: `uv tool run conventional-pre-commit /tmp/msg.txt`. **The same commit produced a second, worse process fault worth the row:** the rejected check was chained `check && commit && push` in one shell call, so a non-zero gate did not stop the commit. The message itself was fine (both correct invocations return 0, so nothing was pushed in violation of the hook config), but the ordering was — a gate whose exit code is consumed by the next command in the pipeline is not a gate. Check, read its exit code, THEN commit, in separate calls.

                                                                                                                            | **Three rulings that shaped the slice, each recorded rather than silently absorbed.** **(1) The plan names `EventFilters` for the verdict filter — that file is DEAD CODE** (zero non-self imports), so the filter landed on the live surface `FilterChips.tsx` instead; recorded per the doc-vs-repo rule. **(2) The plan's first box says "typecheck-red first … generated types regenerate" — nothing regenerated:** the 1.1/1.3 surfaces had already put `verification` and `verdict` on the generated types (the WS schema carried `verification` natively since P0.4), so there was no typecheck red to write. The red lives in the BEHAVIOR, and it went red-first there (307 pins). **(3) The vlm-mode signal for the retired-enrichment empty state is the event's own `verification` row** — spec §4: "verification object, present on every vlm-mode event" + "Legacy events have no verification row", so the row IS the data-driven mode flag; no `PIPELINE_MODE` plumbing was added to the frontend (1.5's setting stays a backend deployment concern). **The null-lie doctrine, where it actually bit:** `risk_score \|\| 0` renders a never-analyzed (or `verification_failed`, D11-NULL) event as a confident green **Low** badge — a reader who trusts it is told an unchecked event was checked and found harmless. `src/utils/risk.ts` gained `resolveRiskLevel(level, score)` (server-computed level WINS — it already carries SeverityService thresholds; score is the fallback; NULL/empty = **no level**, never 0) and `riskSortKey`/`compareRiskSortKey` (unverified ranks ABOVE every score worst-first — a broken verification is an incident, not a dismissal; the naive `key(a)-key(b)` on two Infinities yields NaN and scrambles the whole sort, so two unknowns compare EQUAL — pinned). Applied across the plan's named surfaces + their card twins: `EventListView`, `EventTimeline`, `EventCard`, `MobileEventCard`, `TimeGroupedEvents`, `EventDetailModal`, `severityColors`. **The EventClusterCard case is the instructive one:** 6 pins broke my first attempt because I recomputed the header from member events, but the producer (`eventClustering.ts`) publishes `highestRiskScore`/`highestRiskLevel` as the cluster's OWN contract and the tests override them — the lie lives in the PRODUCER's default (an all-unscored pile → `(0,'low')`), so the component gates on the producer's own scoredness condition (`events.some(score != null)`) and keeps the summary contract. **Verdict badge:** one owner `common/VerdictBadge` keyed on the VERDICT — the interim ActivityFeed chip keyed on `risk_score === null` was blind twice (a SCORED `rejected` event showed no chip; `verification_failed`'s NULL score looked identical to never-analyzed). `VERDICT_CHIP_FILTERS` exports the filter vocabulary from the same LABELS table so chip and badge cannot drift spellings; the glance surface keeps its restraint (a `confirmed` event with a score shows only its risk badge — a green chip on every card is noise). **The verdict filter needed a backend half:** the verdict lives ONLY on `event_verifications`, never denormalized onto Event, so `GET /events` + `/search` gained an EXISTS/NOT-EXISTS filter applied to the BASE query (an unfiltered total above a filtered list is a lying count), `'none'` as a first-class pseudo-value for never-verified (the first question an operator asks a fresh vlm deploy; the CHECK still refuses it as a STORED value — deliberate asymmetry, pinned), and the param is a `Literal` so a typo is a **422, never an empty list** (an empty list reads as "no rejected events" — the same false all-clear 1.5's no-silent-fallback rule refuses for `PIPELINE_MODE`). **Deferred, honestly named:** `AlertsPage.tsx`/`AlertCameraGroup.tsx` still carry `\|\| 0` sites — the plan names only EventTimeline/EventListView, and the alerts surfaces are a separate payload shape (flat alert rows, not events); not silently fixed, not silently skipped. **R8 honored:** nothing deleted — the retired enrichment panels gained an empty state, and `model_management.py`'s retired-model docstrings from 1.4 point at exactly this pattern. survives inside the Serving VLM section, still true in kind); **`legacy_llm` - prod compose's ai-llm has no `profiles:` block (`:120`), so `--profile vlm up` starts the retired 30B path ALONGSIDE ai-vlm** and one A5500 cannot serve both (F10 keeps the service, not its deployment - the handout says keep it out of the up set); plus CDI `gpu=all`, the `TMPDIR` trap at `:57`, and 12 stale `.pyc` (flagged, not deleted, R8). **How the checks are built matters:** the ai-vlm read is **block-scoped** (service-block parse, not a flat line scan), because the legacy ai-llm legitimately mounts the 30B dir and derives `MODEL_PATH` from `LLM_MODEL_PATH` - a flat scan false-reddens the VLM checks on the retired service's own lines (pinned), and only a mount whose **target is exactly `/models`** counts, so the `/models/model-zoo` mounts are not misread as the weights mount (pinned). A `MOUNT_RE` shortest-host regex handles `${AI_MODELS_PATH:-/export/ai_models}`, whose own `:-` colon breaks a naive `.split(":")`. **Two bugs found in my own red suite, not in the green code:** a `profile-gated ai-llm` case that replaced into a fixture with no ai-llm at all, and a `.replace("CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=86", "=89")` that blanked the key and produced the empty-value WARN instead of the 89 FAIL - both fixed in the test, each a silent-vacuous-pin class of error. `2026-09-25-a5500-bringup-checklist.md` got a SUPERSEDED banner rather than deletion (R8: build empty states, not deletions) so an A5500 reader cannot land on Nano-4B instructions. **The run itself is the owner's [O]:** smoke-serve Qwen3-VL-4B and run the P0.3 enforcement probe BEFORE any event reaches the server - the ordering the handout states as mandatory and the precheck can only assert about the tree, never about the box. | M1 review — the seven fixes (the null-lie, the provenance readers, the vlm wire, the prompt budget) | **CLOSED [V]** — the milestone's built-in code review (the goal file retires `superpowers:`/`codex` here), executed over the Phase 1 vlm path and turned into seven commits, each red-first, each gated. **Scope honesty:** this review is SELF-verified, not agent-verified — one parallel vlm-path agent's residency claim was disproved and is corrected at item 24                                                                                  | 2026-09-27 | `uv run pytest backend/tests/unit/ -n auto` → **1 failed, 30,433 passed, 123 skipped, 8 xfailed** (sole failure `test_deploy_phases::test_skips_when_skip_build`, the sanctioned `systemctl` env-fail, identity unchanged from the 1.1/1.3b baselines); `uv run pytest backend/tests/contracts/ -n0 --timeout=30` → **646 passed**; `uv run python scripts/gen-ai-contract.py --check` → "AI contract is current"; `cd frontend && npm run typecheck` → exit 0; per-slice `npx vitest run src/...` from `frontend` (8 suites 229 tests; search suites 111 passed / 24 pre-existing skips)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **The review's shape: every finding was a rule that shipped with a READER that ignored it.** Seven classes, all fixed at the reader, never at the caller, because a caller that forgets is the failure mode the rule exists for. **① `c8050bac` provenance (F11):** `match_face()` gained `model_id` + the canonical outcome table — an untrusted probe (None or the sentinel) and a foreign-space row are both refused a number, and a gallery where a foreign row is the byte-better match no longer reports a match (skipped and counted). The face leg reads the belt off the LOADED handle inside the gallery seam (an unload race reads None → unavailable, never a guess); an uncomparable gallery degrades the whole specialist line instead of folding into "1 unknown face" (an uncomparable gallery is not an observation about a person); `POST /face-events/match` retired **410 Gone** — the D-1 precedent applied to the face twin, with OpenAPI + generated TS in the same commit; the entities route threads the query's `model_id` into `find_matching_entities`, which could previously only return a permanently empty 200; and enrollment's two "no crop" arms were conflated by nullable columns — a zero-AREA bbox is corrupt data (raises), a detection with no bbox at all is the legitimate weak path (logged, used whole). **② `4bc16ebe` the null-lie (D11) on the surfaces 1.6 did not name:** `utils/risk.ts`'s null-safe helpers had shipped with **zero production adopters**, so D11 was enforced only in its own unit tests while the live mappers still coalesced — `DashboardPage`'s `risk_score ?? 0` erased the nulls the P0.25 gauge guard then filtered on (dead code for every REST event; an unscored newest event read as "all clear", pinned red as `data-risk-score="0"`), `useConnectionStatus` folded invalid levels to `low`, and `useTopEventsQuery`/`TopEventsCarousel` — the surface whose job is "show me the worst" — buried never-verified events beside harmless ones (`?? 0`) and NaN'd on two unknowns (`Infinity - Infinity`). Now: nulls pass through, ranking goes through `compareRiskSortKey`, an unscored tile renders the verdict badge on a neutral border. **③ `2ea790bc` the vlm CLIENT wire (the mode was nonfunctional end-to-end):** compose set `PIPELINE_MODE=vlm` and passed `NEMOTRON_URL`/`AI_GATEWAY_URL`/`YOLO26_URL` but NO vlm client var ever reached the backend `environment:` block, and no compose file in the repo declares `env_file:` (verified: zero occurrences) — so `Settings`' dev default won inside a container that shares no network namespace with ai-vlm and `ai_vlm_url` stayed `http://localhost:8098` = the backend's own loopback. Every `vlm_assess` → connection refused → §6's terminal rung → **100% of events `verification_failed` with a NULL score, while the breaker reported a MODEL outage that was a config gap.** The same omission silently no-oped 1.5's per-build enforcement (`vlm_required_build` empty ⇒ the probe's build assertion is skipped), so after any llama-server/GGUF swap the client trusted whatever build answered — enforcement proven against one build laundering onto an unknown one, which is what S-2 exists to prevent. All six vars threaded `${VAR:-default}`; `AI_VLM_URL`'s fallback becomes the service DNS name; `VLM_REQUIRED_BUILD` keeps an EMPTY default ON PURPOSE (compose cannot know which build a local build produced — threading is the fix, the default is not). **④ `058ac533`** `SearchResultCard`'s two badges both read `(risk_level as RiskLevel) \|\| 'low'` on nullable fields — the search surface was not in 1.6's file list, so the lie survived there; the level now derives from the score (`utils/risk` is the single source), so no score = no level = "Unverified", and a scored result renders byte-identically. **⑤ `01080b51` the key-frame budget counted the wrong thing:** `select_key_frames` deduped by `detection_id` and ranked `(camera, class)` PAIRS, but a still is a physical file and the detector emits one row per object over a SHARED `file_path` (`detector_client:1184`) — so one JPEG holding person+car+dog is three pairs on one file, and pair ranking handed that single image up to all four slots: the verifier saw one picture while the request claimed four, and genuinely different frames starved out. Worse, `_key_frame_ids` re-derived ids with `{row["file_path"]: row["id"]}` whose LAST write silently won, so `key_frame_detection_ids` (the UI's thumbnail resolver, spec §4) could name detection 13 when the model had been shown 11. Both now come from the selector's own picks and `file_path` is no longer a key on this path. **The Hypothesis lesson:** the property suite could not have caught this — its generator drew `file_path` from `st.text()`, a unique path per row, the one shape no real batch has; the generator now draws from a small pool so the collision class stays coverable. **⑥ `bdb55335` a clip was being base64'd into the prompt:** `_image_parts` trusted `mimetypes.guess_type(...)[0] or "image/jpeg"`, so a `.mp4` was labelled `image/jpeg` and embedded whole — and this is reachable BY DESIGN, because for video the detector sets `detection_file_path = video_path` (`detector_client:1174`), so every row of a video frame carries the clip and the selector offers it as the batch's one still. Two silent losses: an oversized container overflows one llama.cpp slot and the breaker reports a MODEL outage for a DATA problem (the 1.2 misdiagnosis from the other side), or the engine accepts it and "verifies" an event from bytes no image decoder ever read. Fixed by refusing on TYPE against the repo's own `IMAGE_MIME_TYPES` allowlist, with a byte guard before the read; `VLM_MAX_IMAGE_BYTES` landed in `.env.example` AND the backend block in the same commit (③'s lesson, and `CLIENT_VARS` now catches a knob that silently reads its code default). **Honest about what it is NOT:** the vlm path has no frame extractor — the detector's extracted JPEGs are deleted after detection (`pipeline_workers:746`), `thumbnail_path` is never populated for these rows, and ffmpeg is not in the backend image — so the refusal names the file and says extraction is what is missing, and the missing extractor is a named follow-up, not a buried one. **⑦ `d1e279fd` the prompt is FITTED to its slot:** `vlm_assess` had no context budget at all — `prompt_text()` serialized `context.detections` whole, so a full batch went out at whatever size it came in; measured with the repo's own counter, ~61 tokens per detection row, so `batch_max_detections=500` is ~30K text tokens against a 16,384-token slot. And the only context-window setting the backend carried was the WRONG one: `nemotron_context_window` derives from `CTX_SIZE`/`PARALLEL`, which the backend block mirrors from the LEGACY ai-llm service (262144/8 = 32,768/slot), while in vlm mode ai-llm is not running at all — ai-vlm starts at `VLM_CTX_SIZE`/`VLM_PARALLEL` (32768/2 = 16,384). Grading a vlm prompt against the legacy figure waves a 16.4K–32.8K prompt into a slot half its assumed size. So `vlm_slot_count` + `vlm_context_window` are new fields reading the vlm pair under separate aliases, same derivation rule, vlm scope. **Truncation is visible and deterministic** (the "log the clamp" doctrine): strongest-confidence rows survive on a TOTAL order (confidence, then lowest id — survivors never depend on arrival order, the same replay-equality rule as the key-frame selector), and the prompt says how many rows were omitted and that the survivors are what the attached frames were picked around — a model shown 60 of 500 rows and told nothing reads the gap as "no further activity", a lie by omission on the one channel §6 reserves for detector evidence. The image reservation is the documented ceiling (`MAX_KEY_FRAMES × 1280` vision tokens, the selector's own constant), never a decode, so a prompt approved here cannot overflow on account of its images. `record_prompt_truncated` fires ONCE, at the wire: `prompt_text` runs twice per batch (wire + the analyzer's stored `Event.llm_prompt`), so a counter inside the renderer would double every event — the renderer returns a flag and `assess` owns the effect, and the stored prompt IS the sent prompt, one code path. RULING 2026-09-24: owner says repaired → CONFIRMED REPAIRED.** Closing evidence, same day: fresh-layer non-cached builds ran to completion — `agent-gpu images` shows BOTH `localhost/agent-vss1/ai-vlm:sm103` (3,588,219,493 B) and the fresh-layer `ai-vlm:sm103-b11090` (b11090 clone+compile, the exact class that used to panic). Run-jobs served fine earlier because they ran as the broker default user. **Unblocked:** the `ai-vlm:sm103` image path (G0.2's sanctioned-build caveat retired) and Phase 1's 1.2 compose wiring.
3. **F3 — G0.3 vlm-mode GPU stack. RULING 2026-09-24: N/A on GB300, step closed.** The step built the mode where the VLM borrows the GPU by _stopping the other GPU models_ (Florence/enrichment under Triton) — needed on the 3090 box where memory was the fight. On this GB300 the spec's own rev-4 figure (~47.9 GiB ours) makes that dance pointless, no arm64/sbsa `tritonserver` base exists in any repo Dockerfile (26.01-py3 is amd64), and the explicit-mode switch would change a 501-asserted public surface (`model_management.py` tests assert it). Nothing built; no code to retire (R8 untouched). This ruling closes G0's last open step.
4. **F4 — legacy-path fail-closed semantics (spec 0.3). RULING 2026-09-24: APPROVED** (owner: "yes"). Changing the LIVE legacy Nemotron path to verification_failed-with-NULL on score-50 is a behavior change on the byte-identical legacy path. Zero code written. Needs explicit owner confirmation before anything lands.

Owner ruling on media (2026-09-24, after the S-3 re-run showed keyword stock cannot build doorbell-plausible INCIDENT scenes): **infrastructure stack first; media content gets replaced tonight (several hours out)** — Commons frames carry the benign half meanwhile. 16. **F11 — specialists resident, and their outputs reach the VLM (owner ruling 2026-09-25) [O].** Written from the owner's host session as spec **rev 6** plus Phase 1 plan amendments, in the commit that adds this row. The owner asked whether Triton is still needed, whether it complicates a future VSS integration, and whether on-demand loading gives the VLM better context. Findings (host-side reads of this tree):

    - **Triton stays.** It hosts the YOLO26 gate and re-ID. VSS is itself Triton-based: RT-VLM's image carries Triton 26.03, RT-CV can use `nvinferserver`, and RT-Embed runs on `tritonserver` (audit 09 §1-§2). So Triton doesn't complicate Phase 4. Co-residency with RT-VLM's fixed vLLM reservation does, and a **static** Triton footprint is the easy neighbour for it.
    - **With one load set left after rev 5, explicit mode has no job.** A static `vlm` model repository with `--model-control-mode=none` gives the same residency, avoids specialist cold starts under S4, and leaves the 501 public surface alone.
    - **Loading policy doesn't create context.** Context comes from what reaches the prompt, and **nothing did**: `vlm_analyzer` defaults `specialist_outputs` to empty, and `AssessInput` had no such field, so live and replay would also have diverged.

    Rulings:

    1. 1.4 is rescoped to the static repository (YOLO26 + re-ID + optional threat). Explicit mode, real load/unload, the contract flip and the specialist wake hook are dropped. Revert `7f7113ed` and discard the uncommitted `lifecycle.py` unless something depends on them.
    2. New 1.3b: face match, plate read, re-ID and the optional threat detector feed `specialist_outputs`. A failure records `unavailable` and never blocks the verdict.
    3. The `AssessInput` freeze is reopened for exactly one optional `specialist_outputs` field, default empty, so the 421 stored items stay valid. Replay never re-runs specialists.
    4. Synthetic items gain verdict-changing specialist cases.
    5. D3 now allows the threat detector as an optional fourth specialist.

    Ruling 1 executed (sandbox session, 2026-09-26) [V]: the explicit-mode artifacts were **discovered mid-flight** (the agent had built the gateway load/unload plumbing before the host-session amendment landed in the work tree), so the "unless something depends on them" check is concrete. Nothing depended on them: no module imported `adapters/lifecycle.py` (written minutes before, uncommitted, unmounted), and `7f7113ed` had exactly two consumers — its own test additions and the adapter being discarded with it. Disposition: `git rm` of the two uncommitted files (no history to rewrite), and `git revert 7f7113ed` → `dd157997` (kept as history rather than a force-push, since it was already pushed to `github`). Post-revert proof: `git diff 27f4bd96` over non-docs paths is EMPTY (code identical to the last fully-green head) and the two gates re-pass — `uv run pytest ai/gateway -q` 248 passed / 1 skipped, `scripts/gen-ai-contract.py --check` "AI contract is current". The contract's `model_preload`/`model_unload` availability and `model_management.py`'s 501s were never touched. What was LEARNED while the explicit-mode design was live stays useful for the static-repository task: no gateway lifecycle endpoint exists today (the Tier-A absence guards at `test_client_conformance.py:1139-1190` still hold — they stayed green untouched); the 14 Triton repo names, the `--model-control-mode=none` flag site (`entrypoint.sh:131`), and the `models.yml` `preload:` flag being dormant-everywhere are all re-verified facts for choosing the repository-filter mechanism.

17. **F12 — specialist model picks, and licenses are not a criterion (owner rulings 2026-09-25) [O].** Written from the owner's host session, together with the Phase 1 plan's F12 amendment (Task 3b) and [`docs/vss-integration/14-specialist-model-research.md`](../vss-integration/14-specialist-model-research.md), in the commit that adds this row. Context: the 3b stop-and-ask reported that the face leg has a gallery and matcher but **no server-side embedding extractor**, and asked for a go or no-go on option A (a `model_zoo` ArcFace loader). The owner also commissioned research on memory-light specialists that add intelligence the VLM lacks. Three host-side agents verified candidates on primary sources (model cards, repos, NGC pages); Perplexity deep research supplied leads only. Doc 14 has the tables, the sources and what is `unverified`.

    Rulings and dispositions:

    1. **Face gap: GO on option A, with conditions.** A loader interface (aligned 112×112 crop in, L2-normalized 512-d out, CPU onnxruntime, absent weights mean `unavailable`). **One embedding space:** enrollment computes gallery vectors server-side, the model id is stored on each embedding, and a model mismatch yields `unavailable (re-enroll)`. **Four outcomes:** household match, `unknown`, `not identifiable` (below a size-and-score gate) and `unavailable`. `unknown` is never emitted for a crop that fails the gate. Models: SCRFD-10G-KPS plus `w600k_r50` from InsightFace `buffalo_l`, only those two ONNX files, hash-pinned. B (detection only) and C (reviving the old enrichment service) are rejected.
    2. **Licenses are not a selection criterion.** Owner, verbatim: "we can use yoloe-26, its fine. dont worry about license problems." AGPL (Ultralytics, including the YOLO26 gate), GPL, CC BY-NC and research-only weights and datasets are acceptable. A license is still flagged only when it restricts the _use_ itself: KPR's Hippocratic License 3.0 (surveillance clauses), and BlazeFace's card, which excludes surveillance and identity use. This ruling **supersedes** the host session's earlier AuraFace-v1 + YuNet face pick. That pick was made under license filtering and was never built.
    3. **YOLOE-26 is approved for a future open-vocabulary specialist slot.** That is a spec change (rev 7), not Phase 1. It is also where weapon evidence returns, as hints gated on overlap with a hand or arm.
    4. **Threat call input for 3b (the call is still 3b's to ledger).** The research recommends **not** including `Subh775/Threat-Detection-YOLOv8n`. Its card shows identical 83.0% recall on every class, an unnamed Roboflow dataset, and a "research and educational purposes" disclaimer. The best published CCTV result is handgun AP50 57.4, dropping to 3.7 across datasets.
    5. **Re-ID must prove one vector space end to end** before it is called ready. The inventory cites CLIP-768 at `reid_service.py:430`; the schemas document 512-d OSNet. A general CLIP/SigLIP embedder is a quality gap: zero-shot SigLIP2 re-ID measured mAP 2.8-14.2% in a 2026 11-model comparison. The swap to a trained re-ID model (PersonViT ViT-S/16 or CLIP-ReID) is a rev 7 item.
    6. **Embedding storage: PostgreSQL, no vector database.** The owner asked whether to run Qdrant or use PostgreSQL; the host answer was adopted with the owner's instruction to "edit the plans with this information". At home scale (a gallery of about 200 vectors, re-ID in the tens of thousands, novelty in the hundreds of thousands at most) Qdrant adds a service and a second copy of the truth, outside FK cascades and retention sweeps. **Add pgvector only when a consumer needs indexed search over more than ~100k vectors.** When it comes, build the extension into the current `postgres:16-alpine` image, or dump and restore. Do **not** swap to the Debian `pgvector/pgvector` image over the existing data directory: musl and glibc collations differ, so text indexes go silently inconsistent. The same slice should consolidate today's five embedding formats (LargeBinary ×3, JSONB ×2) into typed columns with a model id.

    Facts corrected by the research [V on primary sources]: the incumbent OSNet-AIN's "MSMT17 Rank-1 73.3%" is the multi-source **MS+D+C→Market** Rank-1; single-source MSMT17→Market is 70.1. That checkpoint's training includes the withdrawn DukeMTMC. The `fast-alpr` stack pins the **v1** OCR (`cct-xs-v1`), and both models total about 10 MB, not 28 MB. The old face service's "~1.5 GB VRAM" is the `insightface` loader putting all five `buffalo_l` models on the GPU, not the two models the face leg needs.

19. **F14 — the S2 and S3 bars are set: `S2_MAX` = 5%, `S3_MIN` = 90% (owner ruling 2026-09-25) [O].** Owner, verbatim: "use 5% and 90%, record them in the ledger." This answers the spec's `S2_MAX`/`S3_MIN` [?] markers (§1 S2/S3 rows) and the brief's stop-and-ask item "the S2 and S3 bars". Both were set **before any bake-off result exists**: the eval store's `runs` and `results` tables are empty. Written from the owner's host session, in the commit that adds this row.

    Rulings:

    1. **S2: at most 5%** of labeled-benign items at `risk_level` ≥ medium (score ≥ 30 under the default `SEVERITY_LOW_MAX` = 29).
    2. **S3: at least 90%** of labeled incidents at or above their expected minimum level.
    3. **Both bars are judged at one operating point.** Report each candidate's S2 and S3 from the same prompt, thresholds and build. Tuning one bar with one setting and the other with another doesn't count.
    4. **Reporting rule:** give each bar's n, the point estimate, and a 95% Wilson interval. **Pass** means the point estimate meets the bar. Label it **marginal** when the interval straddles the bar. The spec's verdict mix (the `uncertain` rate) stays beside the bars.
    5. **Not adopted:** the host session's optional "every critical incident ≥ medium" sub-bar. It would be an S# change (a spec revision), and the owner didn't take it up.

    Why these numbers (the host session's reasoning, which the owner accepted):

    - **S2 = 5% is about the tightest bar the eval set can resolve.** There are 139 benign items, so one item is 0.72 percentage points. An observed 2% has a 95% interval of 0.7-5.9%, which can't be told apart from 5%. A stricter bar needs more benign items.
    - **In daily terms**, 5% means about 1 false medium-or-higher event per day at 20 benign events/day, and 2.5 per day at 50. Actual notifications are fewer: a `rejected` verdict never notifies, and per-camera thresholds and quiet periods filter further.
    - **S3 = 90% is resolvable:** on 274 incidents, the interval around 90% is 85.9-93.0%. It is deliberately demanding. "At or above expected" counts one band short as a miss, and G0's S-3 test saw Qwen3-VL-4B hedge `uncertain` with low scores. If no candidate reaches 90%, the lever is prompts and specialist context, not the bar.

    Eval-store facts found while setting the bars (read-only query of `$AGENT_GPU_DIR/out/eval-store/eval.sqlite`, 2026-09-25) [V]:

    - **Composition:** 421 items, **139 benign** (134 synthetic + 5 stock) and **282 incidents** (274 synthetic + 8 stock). Every benign item expects `low`. Incident expected minimum levels: 30 critical, 118 high, 126 medium, and **8 low**.
    - **Only 13 of 421 items have media** (the stock items). The 408 synthetic items are born-labeled but still have **no images**; they wait on the owner's synthetic media generation (F9 ruling 2). **Consequence for F13 term 4:** until that media exists, 2.2's bake-off runs cannot measure S2 or S3. 5 benign and 8 incident items resolve nothing. 2.1 (the replay harness) can still be built and proven on the 13 stock items plus fakes. **Owner action:** generate the synthetic media for the 408 items.
    - **8 incidents expect `low`,** so any score satisfies them and they inflate S3 for free. That is a label contradiction for an incident. **Owner decision pending:** relabel them or exclude them from S3. Until it's made, the 2.2 report gives S3 both with the 8 (n = 282) and without them (n = 274).
      **DEFERRED 2026-09-26 (owner, verbatim):** "ignore this for now" — the double-report (S3 with n = 282 and without n = 274) stays the reporting rule until the owner returns to it.
    - **CLOSE POINTER 2026-09-27 (item 33):** this row stays **[O]** until the corpus lands — but the door is now pinned: a born-labeled batch that is media-bearing, attribution-carrying AND specialist-declaring at once freezes through the shipped importer with its labels refused rather than guessed (`bae92f47`, item 33, dry-run at 100/24 through freeze+replay). What remains open is the corpus itself, not what our boundary does with it.

Sentinel `LEGACY_MODEL_ID = "legacy-unknown-provenance"` (no "@") is the untrusted state and is **never scored**; it is also the `person_embeddings.model_id` column DEFAULT, so every pre-swap row lands untrusted — drop-and-re-enroll expressed honestly, not a shim (driver check 8, psql-verified `DEFAULT 'legacy-unknown-provenance'::character varying`).
    **Guards:** `compare_person_vectors` (the pure decision both readers share) scores a row ONLY when its `model_id` equals the probe's — not the threshold, not the bytes — and answers `UNAVAILABLE_REENROLL` when nothing is comparable; `reid_service.cosine_similarity` and `entity_repository._cosine_similarity` keep their own raise/zero behaviors as bug-vs-data signals, and `osnet_loader`'s pad-or-truncate-to-512 became a raise (D-5: a wrong checkpoint never becomes a plausible vector). Redis partitions by producer (`entity_embeddings:{model_id}:{date}`, D-3) with the per-entry belt outranking the key; a beltless/legacy search returns zero candidates, which is the honest "no partition to read", never a cross-space score.
    **API/config changes:** **D-1** the client-vector person-match endpoint is **410 Gone** (a vector computed off-box cannot be trusted to share the space and a client-claimed `model_id` is no trust anchor — the face client-vector enrollment precedent), with `docs/openapi.json` + `frontend/src/types/generated/api.ts` regenerated in the same slice and `npm run typecheck` clean; **D-2** `reid_similarity_threshold` 0.85 → **0.7 PROVISIONAL** (OSNet-space; the CLIP-era 0.85 would silently drop every legitimate OSNet match) and the value now rides ONE shared constant `DEFAULT_SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD` through routes, request-schema Field defaults and the OpenAPI-exposed default, so no surface can regress alone; calibration against real household galleries is the named follow-up, same note as the face thresholds. **D-6** `result.clip_embeddings` → `result.reid_embeddings` (grep-verified zero frontend runtime reads).
    **B5a — vehicles have no embedding producer in the shipped mode (a finding, not a silently-dropped case):** `_run_reid` routed `person` and `vehicle` through the SAME call, but OSNet-AIN is a _person_ model (256×128 person aspect, MSMT17-trained), so a vehicle crop through it is noise — and in vlm residency `/clip` is retired and the residency base set is `(yolo26, reid)`. Vehicle identity therefore keeps riding **plate match**, which is already its primary path (`match_vehicle` tries plate first at similarity 1.0), and `_match_vehicle_visual` degrades exactly as it already did (`RegisteredVehicle.reid_embedding` has no production writer today). The endpoint + schema copy now say so (LEGACY branch, "no shipped producer"), and a vehicle-specific re-ID model (fashion-clip / CLIP-ReID class) is a **named follow-up**; the A3 `model_id` partition already admits a second legitimate space without risking a cross-space score. Also named, not taken here: retiring `/api/reid/search` (kept alive — `model_id=None` answering empty forever IS the F11 degrade, and retiring it is a new API decision), and removing the legacy CLIP-named payload keys (`face_clip`/`vehicle_visual`).

Acceptance is the real-`.pth` proof, not a mocked import: driver check 2 loads the pinned checkpoint through the shipped loader in ~1.0–1.4 s and gets the exact belt + dim 512.

(Both ⑦/⑧ clean up their own rows; the leg's injected gallery lets the driver drive the SHIPPED leg, not a re-implementation.)
    **The real defect the driver found (TDD red→green):** `reid_service` did `list(result.embedding)` straight off the handle, and a numpy `float32` array yields numpy scalars — which `store_embedding`'s atomic Lua write (`json.dumps`, NEM-4474) cannot encode: `TypeError: Object of type float32 is not JSON serializable`. Reproduced at the exact site (element type `float32`) and seen live in the driver's Redis leg. **Swap-introduced class:** the retired CLIP producer arrived JSON-decoded from HTTP (already native floats), so the store path only met numpy when the producer became local inference — and the unit tier missed it because it asserted length, never element type (its `ExtractDouble` already builds a real numpy array, so the fix is meaningful, not circular). Red pin `test_generate_embedding_returns_json_safe_native_floats` (asserts `type(v) is float` and the actual `json.dumps` call), green at the producer's return: `[float(x) for x in result.embedding]`. The other two person-vector producers were verified, not assumed: both already convert via `.tolist()`.

The fake-provider dim identities in the conformance tier did NOT move (that tier pins the gateway/Triton side, which already emitted OSNet-512 — the backend store was the outlier); no `ai_contract` surface moved, `gen-ai-contract.py --check` reports current, no golden regeneration. Diff scope: **63 files, +2337/−1111** (services, API routes/schemas, generated `openapi.json` + `api.ts`, `models.yml`, tests, docs).
    **Collection Sanity fired on the swap head — the increase path, exercised as designed (2026-09-27, fix `61973aeb` [V]).** CI's `Collection Sanity (zero-byte / zero-test tracked files)` went red on `12f78722`: `e28def43`'s real-`.pth` proof added three imperative skips — its honest env gates (pinned weights absent / torch absent / torchreid's tensorboard chain unimportable; every CI env takes all three, every GPU box runs the real thing) — moving the `pytest_skip_imperative` census 96→99, plus anchor drift on four older entries from this branch's own file growth (the STALE/UNREGISTERED pairs). Repair per the WP1.3 doctrine the gate itself names: registry re-minted via the sanctioned `suppression-registry-gen.py` (semantic audit of the diff: exactly +3 entries, zero removals, rest pure anchor re-anchoring with identical payloads), baseline raised 96→99 in the SAME commit, and the census gate's own spec-mirror literal raised with its dated rationale line (b80d00b4 precedent). CI-exact local reproduction before pushing: check-test-collection / flake-allowlist / census --expect / ratchet-check / ai-provider-parity / registry --check all 0, the anti-rot gates' own suites 29 passed. **Tooling erratum:** `suppression-registry-gen.py --only <category>` DROPS every non-empty other category (first attempt lost ~1900 entries — caught by the per-category count audit, reverted); the full-file regen is the correct invocation.
    **Two process lessons this slice earned.** (i) **An exit code from a pipeline is not pytest's.** A run reported `EXIT=0` while its log showed `92 failed, 39 errors` — the `0` came from the trailing `tail`, not pytest. Since then every tier's summary line is captured with `echo "PYTEST_EXIT=$?"` written INTO the log. (ii) **Never run two integration tiers on one Postgres.** Overlapping xdist runs deadlocked on the conftest drift-ALTER (`deadlock detected`, 245×) and produced ~90 FK violations that were cross-worker interference, not code; `pgrep -c pytest` is now checked before an integration run, and the exclusive re-run reproduced the pre-swap identity (4088) exactly, which is what proves those failures were collisions. (iii) carried from the doc sweep: **an agent's self-report of a file edit is not evidence** — two agents in the fan-out claimed edits `git diff` showed never happened (one claimed a code block that exists nowhere in the tree), so every claimed mutation was line-by-line verified against the diff before any of it was kept.

23. **Erratum: compose `prod.yml`'s ai-vlm comment claimed a "~6K prompt" worst case that cannot hold (2026-09-27) [V].** The 1.2 row derived the slot from "4 images × ≤1280 + ~6K text + ~1K output ≈ 12.2K", and that arithmetic is right only for a batch of about **90 rows** — `batch_max_detections` ships at **500** (`config.py:947`), and the repo's own offline token counter measures ~**61 tokens per detection row**, so a max-size batch is ~30K text tokens before a single image. The slot is still the right size, but for a different reason than the comment gives: the CLIENT now FITS the prompt to the slot (`vlm_client._fitted_prompt`, `d1e279fd`) instead of the deployment trusting a hand-computed worst case. Recorded as a dated erratum in the compose comment itself (never a silent spec or comment rewrite, per the doc-vs-repo rule), plus a ledger row here. **The general rule this earns: a per-request budget must be computed from the thing that varies (the batch), never from an assumed batch size.** Any future spec text quoting "~6K text" inherits this erratum.

24. **The vlm-path agent's residency claim was WRONG and is corrected here (2026-09-27) [V].** A parallel agent working the vlm path reported that the OSNet handle is resident at boot as a matter of configuration. It is not, and the check is cheap enough that this row carries it: `settings.backend_model_preload` defaults **False** (`config.py:2859`), so the boot sweep at `main.py:1166` does not run unless the operator turns it on; `setup.py` sets it true only when `total_vram_mb > 24 * 1024` (`:645`, `:861`), which **excludes** the 24 GB A5500 (the target hardware is on the wrong side of a strict inequality on its own spec figure). And the `preload:` flag in a `models.yml` row is parsed into `ModelConfig.preload` (`model_zoo.py:497`) and then **read by nobody** — the boot sweep filters on `enabled and not available`, never on `cfg.preload`, so a row that says `preload: true` preloads nothing and a row that says nothing preloads everything the flag permits. Consequences, all honest: on the A5500 and on any CPU/sandbox deployment the handle is **absent** and both the re-ID leg and enrollment answer **unavailable** — which is the correct degrade by design (`vlm_specialists.py:668` names it), not a silent stub, but it is NOT "resident". Measured on this box (CPU, idle): a cold `load()` ≈ **1.158 s**, a warm extract ≈ **0.11 s**, and a transient `load()` releases its weights at refcount 0, so a per-request load is a per-request ~1.1 s, not a free read. **What closes it is a decision, not a fix:** either the residency policy moves from `setup.py`'s install-time VRAM guess to something the deployment declares, or the sweep gains a real reader for `cfg.preload`; both are outside M1 and neither is silently patched here. **Process note, and the reason this row exists at all: an agent's self-report of repo state is not evidence.** This claim was disproved by reading three files; the same fan-out produced two other claims `git diff` showed never happened. Every agent assertion in this workstream gets a file:line re-check before it reaches a row or a report.

27. **Three verification-discipline notes from the `d1e279fd` slice (2026-09-27) [V].** (i) **A background tier with a 0-byte log is not a pass.** Two background full-tier runs reported exit 0 and wrote empty log files; neither was counted, and the tier was re-run in the FOREGROUND to get a real summary line. The rule generalizes item 22's: the evidence is the printed summary, not the exit code — an exit code with no captured `PYTEST_EXIT=`/`N passed` line proves nothing. (ii) **A transient xdist worker ERROR is an identity to chase, not a green.** The first foreground tier showed an extra `ERROR` on `test_florence_client.py::TestFlorenceUnavailableError::test_error_with_message_only` — a pure exception-construction test with no fixtures and no I/O. It reproduced **0 times** in 3 targeted repeats (florence + the changed suites under `-n auto`, 250 passed each), 0 times standalone (128 passed), and 0 times on a clean second full tier (`1 failed, 30,433 passed … in 73.51 s`, the sole failure the sanctioned env-fail). Banked as a worker artifact under load, with the reproduction counts, so a later session that sees it again has the prior instead of a fresh hunt. (iii) **A syntax reading needs the file's actual bytes and the interpreter's version, not a terminal's rendering:** this repo runs Python **3.14** (`.venv/lib/python3.14/`), where PEP 758 makes `except TypeError, ValueError, AttributeError:` valid without parentheses — the repo's ruff actively normalizes parenthesized except-tuples TO the parenless form, so `config.py:1283` and `:1413` are both correct. (My own first reading of that line in-session — "Python 2 syntax, or a terminal rendering artifact" — was wrong, and it cost a detour; recorded here so the next reader does not repeat it rather than as a correction to any written note, because none was ever written.) When a hook rewrites something I wrote, the first question is what the tool's own rule is, not how to fight it.
28. **The enforcement gate's budget is a per-scene coin flip, and it can open the breaker (2026-09-27) [? — owner decision queued; ledger finding G].** `_PROBE_MAX_TOKENS = 400` was sized for the `/completion` probe, whose grammar is the analyzer schema (4 scalar fields). The shipped CHAT probe sends the full verdict wire schema instead — `description`, a `criteria` array, a `provenance` object — so at 400 the same request echoes the nonce **3/4 times on `break_in_attempt`** (4/4 at 700), `pet_activity` 0/4 at 400 → 4/4 at 700, `loitering` ≈2000. Because only ENFORCED is cached (S-1, correct), a scene sitting on the cliff makes a whole replay's enforcement verdict depend on which image the store yielded first. **Shipped already, so the floor is safe:** a truncated probe now answers `inconclusive` (never `ignored`) and the budget-caused failures stopped feeding the `ai-vlm` breaker — measured before the fix, five verbose scenes opened the breaker and the rest of the corpus was refused WITHOUT I/O, which is finding B's contamination wearing a budget. **What is owed is the ceiling** (owner-level; it trades probe latency/S4 wall against proof, and a probe-contract change is not mine to make): raise the budget (measured need 700 clears 4/6, `loitering` ≈2000), OR probe with a minimal probe-only schema — the CI gate proves enforcement with 4 scalar fields, so requiring the model's whole verdict prose to prove a grammar pays output cost for evidence the probe never reads (my read: this one, with the budget raise as its enabler), OR scope the cache to (endpoint, build) as S-2's wording implies rather than (client, first image). The three 2.2 candidate runs keep their numbers (refusals A 2/13, B 1/13, C 0/13, computed on items that answered), but `ENFORCED` should be read as "enforced on the scene that happened to be first" until one of the three is chosen.

29. **The M1 milestone review's biggest finding was two agents inventing code (2026-09-27) [V].** The built-in review of the `13f2d65e..d1a44c62` range (the Phase 2 harness + the new probes) was fanned out to two agents, and each returned a "CRITICAL" set that does not exist in the tree. **Agent A's label-contradiction finding cited a file that is not in the repo** (`build_gen2.py` — `build_gen2` is a function at `eval_store.py:546`), **a field the schema has never had** (`EvalItem` at `assess_input.py:40-51` carries `expected_label`/`expected_risk_score`, no `expected_min_level`; `git grep expected_min_level -- backend/` is empty), and **`eval_store.py:268`, which is the synthetic label-set path, not the stock one**. **Agent B's two criticals described an order the code does not have:** `enforcement.py:144-149` returns INCONCLUSIVE "HTTP {status} - not measurable" on a non-200 — not `IGNORED`, which is a body judgement reserved for a complete reply without the const — and its const-echo check at `:154-158` runs BEFORE the truncation branch at `:166` (echo → ENFORCED is finding A's pinned order, with a comment naming it), so the whole "budget converts ENFORCED to IGNORED" scenario cannot occur. Its `tool_calls.py:207` "no /props gate" claim is off by eight lines from a `/props` block that starts at `:188`. **What the review did find is one minor, real, non-defect**: `s_metrics.s2_false_positive_rate` counts the benign arm's refusals in `refused` and reports them beside the rate, so a refuse-everything model shows `fp_rate 0.0` and needs the adjacent field to read as an escape (its own docstring says "has not passed S2, it has escaped it", and the bake-off report quotes refusals in the headline). Kept as a reporting-shape observation for 2.2's report generator, not a code change — the harness is honestly labelled already. The agent's follow-up claims went the same way, all checked against the code: S5 "crashes the report on a stored NULL-score row" cannot happen — `compute_report` takes the in-memory rows `replay_item` builds, and both of its paths set `verdict` and `risk_score` (the refused path writes `verification_failed`/None explicitly, and `s5_refusals` uses `.get`); "refusals hide inside S3 as misses" is REAL but safe-direction — a refused incident is counted `not-a-hit`, so a refusing model is scored worse, never better, and the refused count is its own reported field beside it; the tool-probe's "SUPPORTED without an arguments check" inverts `:150-155`, which compares the parsed `token` to the nonce, and its `except: pass` on malformed arguments yields `args=None`, which then fails exactly that comparison. The one thing worth KEEPING from the review is the S2/S3 reporting asymmetry above, plus a candidate-level `run_manifest.json` (endpoint, build, budget, corpus gen per run) for 2.2's report generator, and — this is the meta-finding for how reviews get run here — an agent-reported "critical" in this repo has now been wrong 8 for 8 across two agents, so the rule this review earned is that a finding is only ever acted on after its `file:line` is opened: item 24 said don't trust an agent's claim; this review says treat the CRITICAL label itself as the least trustworthy part of the report. Nothing in the harness changed as a result of the review.

31. **Step 0 (branch hygiene): `github/main` merged in, tiers re-proved — and the banked unit identity turns out not to be a single identity (2026-09-27) [V].** The merge is `e62aa86b`, and it is a MERGE and not a rebase for the reason the goal names: #6681 landed as a squash (`4bfd6fa4`), so the branch's 75 commits are absent from main _by identity_ while main holds one commit absent from the branch — rebasing would duplicate the content; merging is the only shape that converges. `git diff --stat e62aa86b^1 e62aa86b` printed **nothing**, so this step is a verification and not a repair, and the next PR's diff is its own work rather than 75 already-merged commits. **The tiers on the merged tree:** contracts `-n0 --timeout=30` → **646 passed, exit 0** (`/tmp/contracts_step0.log`); `scripts/gen-ai-contract.py --check` current; unit `-n auto` **green modulo the sole sanctioned failure** `test_skips_when_skip_build` in every run — but three runs, three different identities: banked `1 failed, 30544 passed, 124 skipped, 8 xfailed` (`/tmp/unit_item30_tier.log`), then `30545 passed / 123 skipped` (`/tmp/unit_step0.log`, `/tmp/unit_step0_rs.log`), then `30543 passed / 124 skipped, 1 error` (`/tmp/unit_step0_rs2.log`). I refused to call that noise, and diffing the two `-rs` skip lists (`comm` over the `SKIPPED [...]` lines, 111 vs 112 entries) named the ±1 mover exactly one line deep, with a mechanism that makes the **pass** the wrong answer, not the skip:

- **Drift ① — `TestTensorboardImportHazard::test_real_module_wins_when_importable` (`test_osnet_loader.py:1357-1361`) asks a question `sys.modules` cannot answer.** It decides "is the REAL `torch.utils.tensorboard` importable here?" by calling `importlib.import_module("torch.utils.tensorboard")` — but `_ensure_tensorboard_importable()` **inserts its inert stub into `sys.modules` and never removes it** (`osnet_loader.py:114-131`), so once any earlier test in the same xdist worker has exercised the torchreid path (item 22's `TestRealWeightsProof` does, at `:1394`), the import succeeds and returns **the stub**. `real` is then the stub and the test's own assertion `sys.modules[...] is real` is **vacuous — it proves nothing while passing.** Proven two ways, not inferred: the skip-list diff (that one line moved skipped→passed and nothing else moved), and a direct probe in an env where tensorboard is definitively NOT installed — after `_ensure_tensorboard_importable()`, `importlib.import_module("torch.utils.tensorboard")` returns a module and `is`-compares equal to the stub (`is-stub: True`). So the test's _skip path_ is destroyed by worker ordering and its _pass path_ is unfalsifiable whenever it runs. **Fix shape, red-first, in item 1's blast radius** (it is the loader item 1 touches): the gate must ask about the `tensorboard` _package_, not about a `torch.utils` submodule the shim may have filled, and the loader's stub — which is correct in production, where it is the only way torchreid imports — must not be the thing a test trusts.
- **Drift ② — an xdist-order-dependent teardown ERROR, `RuntimeError: Event loop is closed`.** `test_florence_client.py:52`'s `reset_global_client` fixture → `reset_florence_client()` (`florence_client.py:1630`) → `.close()` (`:395`) → httpx → httpcore → `asyncio.selector_events:1216 close` → `base_events:550 _check_closed` **raises**, surfacing as `ERROR at setup of TestCircuitBreakerIntegration.test_circuit_breaker_uses_config_settings` — a test whose subject is not the Florence client at all. The file was last touched by `610882b4` (2026-09-22), so it is **not** fallout from this merge; it is a shared-client teardown racing a worker's already-closed loop. Same fix family: close the client before the loop dies, or don't close a client the fixture didn't open.
- **What I did NOT do: no product code rode on this.** Both are test-isolation defects, neither is a shipped behavior, and the sanctioned-failure identity held in all three runs. But the honest restatement of the gate for every later slice is now narrower and has to be said out loud: **"1 failed, 30544 passed, 124 skipped, 8 xfailed" is not a stable identity on this repo** — it drifts by ±1 skipped with xdist ordering (①) and can carry +1 error (②). The claimable invariant is _no failures other than the sanctioned `test_skips_when_skip_build`_ plus a named reason for any count movement, which is what item 27's "the evidence is the printed summary" was always reaching for. Restoring a truly stable identity is the two fixes above, and they land with item 1 rather than as a drive-by.

32. **Item 1 RESIDENCY FIX shipped red-first — closes item 24 by pointer (2026-09-27, owner ruling ">= + cfg.preload reader") [V].** Item 24 ended "what closes it is a decision, not a fix"; the owner decided the shape, and the fix is small because the decision carried it. **The defects, re-derived from the tree before touching it** (item 25's anchors may have moved — they had: `vlm_specialists.py` is 948 lines now, not 1044): (a) `config.py` `backend_model_preload` defaults False; (b) `setup.py` computed `model_preload = total_vram_mb > 24 * 1024` in **three byte-identical inline copies** — strict `>`, so a 24576 MiB card lost on its own spec figure; (c) `main.py`'s sweep filtered `cfg.enabled and not cfg.available` — and `available` flips True only AFTER a successful load, so at boot it selected every enabled row with the flag on and nothing with it off, while `cfg.preload` was parsed and read by nobody; (d) every description along the way ("Eagerly load all AI models", "load all enabled") described a mechanism none of the three sites implemented. **The fix:** one `should_preload_models(total_vram_mb)` in `setup_lib/nvidia_detect.py` (where GPU knowledge already lives) with the INCLUSIVE threshold and a `PRELOAD_MIN_VRAM_MB` constant, replacing all three inline copies (a pin asserts no inline `> 24 * 1024` survives and that all three mode functions call the helper); `select_preload_candidates(model_zoo, *, preload_enabled)` in `main.py` as the single row-selection point (`enabled AND cfg.preload AND not available`), called by the lifespan sweep; `osnet-ain-x1-0`, `face-detector-scrfd`, `face-recognizer` now declare `preload: true` with comments naming F11 — those three are exactly the rows whose handles are read from the manager registry by membership (`get_reid_handle`, `get_face_leg_handles`, which needs BOTH face rows); the plate leg deliberately stays out (it self-loads via `fast_alpr_loader`/the optional `[alpr]` extra, not the zoo). `smoke-fire-yolov8n` already declared `preload: true` and now means it. Config field, `.env` comment and the `models.yml` legend rewritten to describe what the code does. **Two findings the [V] driver earned, both ledgered-not-fixed (scope honest):** ① `face_recognizer_loader` and `smoke_fire_loader` have **no NEM-4501 `validate_model_path` call at all** — osnet's loader does, and it refused my dev-box zoo root (`$AGENT_GPU_DIR/models/model-zoo` isn't under the `security.py:41` allowlist; the shipped root `/export/ai_models` is, and `test_osnet_loader.py:1400-1409` already documents exactly that dev-vs-shipped posture). The asymmetry means the face rows load weights through unvalidated paths today; it predates this change, and adding validation there is its own red-first slice, not a drive-by inside this one. ② `never_evict`/`priority` are still honored by nobody — the model zoo has **no eviction pass at all** (grep: no `def *evict*` outside tests); the legend now says so instead of promising it. **Red-first, 14 pins, first draft `/tmp/item1_red.log`:** 7 in `test_nvidia_detect.py` (helper exists; 24576 is True; 24575 False; above True; 0 False; two-12-GB cards sum True; no inline copy left), 4 in `test_main.py` (flag off ⇒ []; flag on ⇒ only preload rows; already-available row not reselected; the lifespan calls the helper and the old predicate is gone from the source), 3 in `test_model_zoo.py` (the F11 rows declare preload true; the shipped zoo through the real parser selects exactly the declared rows — **derived from the yaml, not a hand-copied list, because my first version of this pin asserted a literal that reality contradicted the moment I ran it**: smoke-fire's loader registers here, so the shipped set is four rows, and the test comment now records that) plus flag-off-on-real-zoo ⇒ []. All 11 first-draft tests failed on the pre-fix tree before any implementation existed. **The [V] driver** (`/tmp/item1_driver.py`, real loads, CPU legs): flag off ⇒ `selected: []`, `reid_handle_resident: false`, `face_handles_resident: false`, registry 0 — F11 reproduced on this box; flag on ⇒ selected the four declared rows, **reid resident with model_id `osnet-ain-x1-0@osnet_ain_x1_0_msmt17@8a07e8da3894`** (byte-identical to the shipped suite's `OSNET_ID` pin at `test_osnet_loader.py:1163` — the sha-enforced belt survives the boot path), face handles resident, registry 3, **whole sweep 1.37 s**; smoke-fire errored honestly (weights absent on this dev box — F12's degrade: sweep warns, boot proceeds, the other three loaded). That is the ~1.16 s cold-load-per-request answered once at boot. **Gate identities from captured logs on this tree:** unit `-n auto -p no:randomly` → `1 failed, 30559 passed, 123 skipped, 8 xfailed` (UNIT_EXIT=1, sole failure the sanctioned `test_skips_when_skip_build`; arithmetic exact: 30544 + 14 new + 1 known tensorboard skip→pass = 30559, 124 − 1 = 123 — item 31's drift, named, not smoothed); contracts `-n0 --timeout=30` → 646 passed exit 0; `gen-ai-contract --check` current; integration with PG/Redis connect-checked in-session (psycopg 16.15 line + Redis PONG logged before the run) → `4089 passed, 107 skipped, 2 xfailed`, INTEGRATION_EXIT=0 — the identity held even though the integration tier boots the app and exercises the changed lifespan. Gate churn: ruff auto-collapsed one signature line, detect-secrets re-baselined one pre-existing `models.yml` entry's line number (111→116, my legend edit shifted it; no new detection) — re-staged to zero churn, 17 hooks pass. **Process correction recorded where it belongs:** item 29's rule turned out to describe me during this same slice, twice — I drafted a `main.py` docstring for a helper I had not yet written (the Edit failed loudly; caught because I re-read the diff instead of trusting the plan) and I "fixed" three `ledger item 1` references that named an item that doesn't exist (now 32).
