33. **Item 2 SYNTHETIC-EVAL INTAKE: the born-labeled contract is pinned at our boundary, and the corpus directory is now the label's ONLY authority (2026-09-27, code `bae92f47`) [V].** The parallel agent builds the generator; the freeze/replay side is ours, so what this slice owns is the DOOR — what a batch must look like to enter, and what refuses. Three defects, all measured on the tree before touching it. **15 new pins total; 9 demonstrated red before their implementation** (`/tmp/item2_red.log`: 6 failed / 5 passed on the pre-fix tree — the 5 greens are guards that prove a refusal does not overreach: no-declaration-still-uses-placement, agreeing-declaration-accepted, the full 413-set corpus still loading, the D10 raise-not-skip, count-identity; +1 red = the specialist pin; +2 red by guard-removal, below).

    - **Defect ① — a label could be flipped by the file that carried it.** `load_synthetic_items` resolved `expected_label` as `_CATEGORY_LABELS.get(labels.get("category", category), category)`: the SET'S DECLARATION was the lookup key and the directory was only a fallback, so a set placed under `threats/` declaring `"normal"` froze **benign** (probed: `synthetic:threats:set_a -> benign`), and a placement outside `{normal, suspicious, threats}` made the DIRECTORY NAME the label — `"threats"` is not a value any reader counts, and `m0_size_report` correctly reports such rows as `unknown` on neither side. `import_generated_items` (:452-454) carried the same conflict bug behind a comment claiming parity with the loader, and its unknown-placement case SKIPPED — a batch the generator mis-folded into one wrong directory imported as zero items and read as an empty-but-green run. Measured first: of the 413 committed sets **366 agree, 47 declare nothing, 0 conflict, 0 out-of-vocabulary**, so refusing is free.
    - **The fix — `resolve_category_label(placement, declared, *, origin)` in `eval_store.py`, the one authority both doors call.** Placement rules; a declaration may CONFIRM it; a conflict or an out-of-vocabulary name raises naming BOTH sides ("sits under 'normal' but declares category 'threats'") — "bad category" alone is not actionable by whoever generated the set. `import_generated_items` converts that refusal to `LabelImportError`, which is already this importer's shape for a misconfigured run (its D10 branch), so a disagreeing corpus aborts instead of landing in a manifest as a loss. Why refuse rather than pick a precedence: a flipped label is invisible downstream and bends the S2/S3 denominators — an incident counted benign deflates the exact side S2 ranks incidents against. Same two-sources-of-truth shape as item 24, same resolution.
    - **Defect ② — the door the parallel agent's batch actually uses dropped `specialist_outputs`.** `load_synthetic_items` and `load_stock_items` both render a declared `specialist_context` through the shipped renderer; `import_generated_items` — the MEDIA-bearing, born-labeled path — built its `AssessInput` without the field. `build_gen2` refuses a generation where zero items carry a rendered block ("the replay measures a system 1.3b did not ship"), but that guard lives on the builder, and this importer writes straight into a store: the same corpus, same replay, opposite outcome depending on the door, silently. Now both doors call `_render_specialist_outputs`; pinned by freezing a set that declares a `person_reid` match and reading `"DryRun"` back out of the STORED item.
    - **Defect ③ — attribution is now required beside every frame, not asserted by the generator's report.** The shipped writer already works this way (`scripts/synthetic_media.py` writes one attribution JSON per frame; `load_stock_items` documents the same for stock), so the intake contract matches it instead of inventing one: `_attribution_gap` skips a set LOUDLY when a frame's sidecar is missing, unparseable, or lacks `license`+`artist` — a 0-byte sidecar is a MISSING one wearing a filename, which is item 27's rule applied to attribution. Loud skip rather than refusal because the size bar below already turns a wholesale-missing batch into a failed gate, so the per-set loss stays visible in the manifest without aborting a run over one bare set. (The `load_stock_items` docstring promises attribution "not inside the item"; until this slice nothing ENFORCED it on the generated path.)
    - **Count-identity block.** `m0_size_report` was already honest (out-of-vocab label → `unknown`, counted beside the bar, never laundered as "unlabeled") — the loader was the bug, and the pin now says so directly: 100 benign + 20 incident + 50 `"threats"`-labeled rows reports `(100, 20)`, `unknown == 50`, `unlabeled == 0`, and the three buckets sum to 170. That is the property the bar rests on: a batch of 100 mis-labeled items must never read as 100 benign.
    - **Why the bars cannot rank (the goal's point, verified not repeated):** the Phase 2 bake-off table (`docs/plans/2026-09-27-vss-phase2-bakeoff-report.md`) ran every candidate at **S2 n=5** and **S3 n=8** — Wilson intervals [0.000, 0.434] and [0.529, 0.978] for identical 0 %/0.875 readings, i.e. three candidates indistinguishable because the media-bearing set is 13 items. The `>=100 benign / >=20 incident` bar is what turns those denominators into measurements; the dry-run above is the first batch that would satisfy it.
    - **The [V] DRY-RUN, one batch end to end through the shipped functions only (`/tmp/item2_dryrun.py`, `/tmp/item2_dryrun_final.log`, aggregate output only — D10).** 124 born-labeled media-bearing sets (100 `normal` / 24 `threats`), off-repo under `$AGENT_GPU_DIR/out/eval-store/item2-dryrun` (`corpus_root_is_off_repo: true`), each frame carrying an attribution sidecar; FROZEN by `import_generated_items` (attempted 124 / imported 124 / skipped 0, `every_row_born_labeled: true`, `media_is_frames_only_not_sidecars: true` — the manifest's attribution rows are copied into the manifest, the sidecar JSONs never become media), REPLAYED by `run_replay` through the shipped `make_client` seam (`vlm_replay.py:52` FakeClient pattern; `vlm_url_source: "injected"`), client answering by `request.image_paths[0]`. Result: `m0_bar` **benign 100 / incidents 24 / m0_complete true**; `specialist` **124 of 124** frozen items carry a rendered `person_reid` line; replay `n_items` 124, **S2 denominator 100** (fp_rate 0.0, refused 0, skipped 0), **S3 n 24** (recall 1.0), 124 stored `results` rows, 124/124 requests carried the specialist block, client closed once; `count_identity.all_agree: true` (importer labels == store payloads == the replay report's `corpus.labels` == `m0_size_report`); negative leg: a `normal/` set declaring `"threats"` → `{"refused": true}` with both sides named, and the good store untouched at 124 items. The S2/S3 numbers come from a SANE fake oracle — this leg proves plumbing and denominators at scale, not a model; the real numbers are the GPU rows. Note this is a DRY-RUN artifact under the off-repo eval root, not a generation: it is not gen-3, and no report was committed with it.
    - **Blast radius, measured:** `import_generated_items` and `resolve_category_label` have **zero importers outside `backend/evaluation/` and its tests** (grep: no app module imports `evaluation.eval_store` / `evaluation.label_import` at all), so no shipped request path changes behavior; the eval tier is the surface, and the full unit tier is the gate anyway. The `tests/unit/evaluation/_make_corpus` fixture now writes a sidecar per frame — it stands in for the generator's output, so making it match the shipped writer is the honest move (that is what turned the attribution pins red: with the guard removed they fail `2 failed, 6 passed`, restored → green, verified rather than assumed).
    - **Gate identities from captured logs on the committed tree (`bae92f47`):** unit `-n auto` → **`1 failed, 30574 passed, 123 skipped, 8 xfailed`, UNIT_EXIT=1**, sole failure the sanctioned `test_skips_when_skip_build` (`/tmp/item2_unit_final.log`; arithmetic exact — item 1's tree printed 30559, +15 new pins = 30574; the skipped count is item 31's named ±1 drift); contracts `-n0 --timeout=30` → **646 passed, exit 0** (`/tmp/item2_contracts_final.log`); `scripts/gen-ai-contract.py --check` → "AI contract is current", exit 0 (no `ai_contract` surface moves — this slice touches only offline eval tooling); the eval tier itself: `287 passed, 3 skipped` (`/tmp/item2_eval_tier.log`). Integration was NOT re-run: nothing this slice changes is reachable from the app (the grep above), and I am not claiming a tier I did not run.
    - **Item 27's rule caught me again, from the timing side [V].** The first rerun "on the committed tree" launched while two behavior-neutral edits (a docstring truthfulness fix — it claimed a license reading `"unknown"` would be rejected when the code only checks truthiness — and an unused-parameter cleanup) were still landing; its unit log printed `11 failed`, ten of them in `test_label_import.py`, because the workers imported the module mid-rewrite. `stat` named it: log finished 15:03:05, commit landed 15:03:30 — the log tested a tree that never existed as a commit. The identities above come from a rerun launched AFTER the commit with a clean `git status`; had I pasted the racy one, I would have disclosed a failure that was an artifact of my own timing. New memory: freeze the tree first, prove second; compare a background log's mtime to the commit time before citing it.
    - **Issue 19 stays [O] and this slice is the reason it can close when the corpus lands.** Item 19's sharpest restatement is this file's finding D: the media-bearing sets and the specialist-context sets are DISJOINT — 13 media-bearing items carry 0 rendered blocks, the 5 with blocks carry 0 media — so the shipped wire could never exercise the specialist prompt. The three fixes above are what let a real batch fix that in one freeze: born-labeled, media-bearing, attribution-carrying, and specialist-declaring AT THE SAME TIME, with the labels refused rather than guessed if the generator ever disagrees with itself. Until a real corpus arrives, 19 remains open and the numbers above are contract evidence, not measurements of the world.

The MoE geometry finding came free: the 30B spends **3072 MiB KV** at the same ctx/parallel where A/C spent 4608 — B's hybrid-class KV relief has company.

Item 29's doctrine applied to my own probe set: the enforcement probe's lone ENFORCED pass had slipped through the same ~25 % window the s2 arm-B kept missing — **intermittency is why a one-shot probe is not a line**; three s2 repeats (ENFORCED / INCONCLUSIVE / INCONCLUSIVE) named it. 36. **A5500 RUN READINESS AUDIT: the handout could not execute the run it exists to drive, and the audit's most valuable finding is a bar it never asked for (2026-09-28) [V].** Asked before the owner boots the box, not after: _"if I bring the A5500 online, is there documentation or a branch we can point it at?"_ The branch was always the right answer; the **handout was not**, and that is the kind of finding that costs an afternoon on a 24 GB machine instead of an hour here. Method: a read-only fan-out (five cold readers + one completeness critic) over the handout, its generator, the spec's bars, the compose and image facts, and fresh-box prerequisites — then **every blocking claim re-verified by me in the file before acting on it**, because an audit's `file:line` is a claim too. What it found, all confirmed: `grep -ic S4` and `-ic p95` were **zero in both the handout and its generator**, on the one box the spec assigns S4 to (spec `:86`, `:388`); S1 was requested as a bare verb with no 20.4 GiB bar, no resident set, no sampler — and `VLM_GPU_LAYERS` defaults to `auto` (`docker-compose.prod.yml:269`), so an over-budget serve spills to CPU RAM, looks healthy, and can post a **fake S1 pass**; the mandatory P0.3 probe was prescribed as a one-shot **"refusal check"** when `scripts/vlm_probes/enforcement.py` is a nonce-const grammar check whose own docstring warns that a wall of refusals must not be read as the verdict — and finding ① is exactly a lone ENFORCED that slipped an intermittently-failing endpoint; there is **no image to pull** (no `image:` key, no ghcr presence, no `VLM_IMAGE`, and `deploy.yml` never builds `ai-vlm`) so the box must build, while the shipped `CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=89` is not a superset of 86 and nothing in `ai/` carries `+PTX`; and the up the handout prescribed was **physically unexecutable** — `backend` carries `depends_on: ai-llm: condition: service_healthy` (`:708-717`), `ai-llm` is unprofiled (`:120`), and `compose config --services` under `--profile vlm` resolves **20 services including `ai-llm`**, so "keep it out of the up set" was prose with no implementation.

    - **What was fixed, and the two things deliberately NOT fixed.** Fixed in the generator (`AMENDMENTS` + re-render — the `.md` is generated, so editing it is editing an artifact): S1's bar/sampler/no-offload trap, a full S4 row with its two cold-start cases, the probe named + invoked + `N>=3` + relabeled, the tool-calling probe (spec `:379` assigns it here and flip condition (ii) waits on it), the build command with the nothing-to-pull fact and the arch-blind-provenance warning, a weights row for a **cold** box (both pairs, bytes, sha256s, target dir, finding F's `chmod 644`), the M1 proof chain from plan `:181`, the Triton/gateway static-repository row and the Florence clause that had been dropped between spec and handout, a new "Environment and first run" section (the two FAILs are the pre-fix state; `--env-file` defaults to the _template_, not the box), and `env-templates/a5500.env.template` (new file — the repo had only a GB300 one pinning arch 103, and `.env.example` cannot even render compose: `PODMAN_SOCKET` `:?` at `:1508`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` `:?` at `:69`). **Not fixed, on purpose:** (a) `ai-llm` was NOT given a `profiles:` block — compose errors when `depends_on` names a service in a disabled profile, so it would break every plain `up`, which is an owner call about prod compose, not a side effect to slip into a bring-up slice; the handout now carries the repo's own proven answer instead, the GB300 bootstrap's two-phase `up -d --no-deps backend frontend` (`scripts/bootstrap-gb300.sh:270-277`, commented "never plain `up` — GPU deps"), verified against `config --services`. (b) `scripts/platform-healthcheck.py` probes **only** `127.0.0.1:8091` — the retired service's `/health`, `/v1/models`, `/slots` (`:172`, `:178`, `:189`, `:440`) — and has no `ai-vlm`/8098 branch, so on a vlm-only box it can fault the service that correctly isn't running and cannot see the one that is. That is a real gap and is recorded with a check-`/props`-directly workaround rather than fixed: rewriting a shared probe mid-run moves the ground under the run being measured.

    - **Three corrections to my own recent work, found by looking.** (a) **`the weights are NOT what the A5500 lacks`** (written into the handout yesterday) is true only of the GB300's broker share `$AGENT_GPU_DIR/models/vlm`, which exists solely behind `agent-gpu` on the dev box — false of a cold A5500, which has no weights at all; the row now says so and carries the pins. (b) **The 4B fallback pair had no sha256 anywhere in the repo** even though this run is _required_ to serve it: `Qwen3VL-4B-Instruct-Q4_K_M.gguf` 2,497,281,664 B sha256 `66358cb18bb6b3b1b6675aa412c7a88ef01d228f481184d13668e5201c730a0a` + `mmproj-Qwen3VL-4B-Instruct-Q8_0.gguf` 453,974,304 B sha256 `30ba2c7dd3127a4561b6cba9d13d0f711c91bdb38742e2f56d73c8cb596bd06d`, measured on the share today. (c) **My own "~2x" KV conflict from `54141d9c` picked a side of an unstated basis.** The report says "4608 MiB / 16384 cells" without saying whether that is one slot's KV or the whole pool expressed per 16K; read per-slot it is ~4× the old compose prose, read per-pool ~2×. Both the compose comment and the handout row now state the spread and name the ambiguity, and assert only what the report's own header supports — that 4B and 8B share the KV row, so the cost does not move with the fallback.

    - **A pin that was green on the broken tree, caught by my own test.** Nine pins were written first for the render (the render is what the operator reads), and **eight went red** — but the build pin passed pre-fix, and the reason matters: `--build-arg CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=86` already appeared inside `_check_vlm_image`'s PASS detail, so a substring check was green while the handout still printed no actual invocation. That is the same self-referential-pin pattern this file flagged at item 35(a), reproduced in my own work the same day; the pin was tightened to require the real command and the nothing-to-pull fact rather than relaxed. The handout is also the second place this repo has been bitten by **prettier pairing mid-word underscores** — `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=${GPU_*}` ships as `CUDA*VISIBLE_DEVICES=${GPU*\*}` and `Q4_K_M` as `Q4*K_M` in the committed file, which is load-bearing here because an operator types what the page says. Proven empirically with a scratch file (corrupted without the ignore entry, intact with it), then both handouts were added to `.prettierignore` following the `archive/` precedent already documented there.

    - **Stale anchors, found not inherited** (item 25's warning demonstrated a third time): the handout's `spec :482-500` for the no-legacy-LLM rule (that range is M3 and Phase 4; the row is at `:523`, plus `:412`), and `.env.example`/compose line anchors off by 25–30 because `.env.example` grew ~25 lines in this slice-set. Sites inside my own new text cite **variable and service names** instead of line numbers, and the ones kept (`compose:240`, `:253`, `:265-266`, `:708-717`, `:1508`, `:69`, `Dockerfile:133`, `bootstrap-gb300.sh:270-277`) were re-read today.

    - **Gates (executed, this tree):** precheck suite **57 passed** (48 at head + 9 new); TDD order real — 8 red, then green, then one pin tightened after it was found green-on-broken. Render verified after every edit and the eight load-bearing operator strings checked in the committed file (`20.4 GiB`, `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=memory.used`, `--no-deps backend frontend`, `podman build --build-arg CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=86`, `enforcement.py --url`, `N>=3`, `chmod 644`, `uv run python`, clean `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=${GPU_*}`) with **zero** corruption patterns remaining. `gen-ai-contract --check` current; contracts 646 and integration 5 passed were established for this branch earlier today and no code path they cover moved here (`docker-compose.yml` comment prose and precheck/handout text only).

    **CLOSE POINTER: this item is [V] — closed as an audit — and it does NOT close M1 or item 35; it is what makes the run that closes them executable.** Still owner-owned: the run itself, and the decision on whether `ai-llm` should ever be profile-gated in prod compose. Still open in the repo, reported not fixed: `platform-healthcheck` has no vlm branch, the spec's `S2`/`S3` rows keep their `[?]` (an `S#` edit is the owner's), and Phase 2's plan `:117` still points at the A5500 checklist as the source of S1/S4 — a pointer that was incomplete until today and is now honest.

37. **THE OPERATOR HANDOFF: what the run needed was an ORDER, not more facts, and writing it caught two hazards of my own making (2026-09-28) [V].** The owner's instruction was "fix whatever you can before I bring it online … push an updated version of the branch and a handoff document we can just give the agent operating on that machine." Item 36 had fixed what the checklist _says_; this slice adds the thing it never had, which is the sequence a cold box walks — because a document organized by spec row is not a document an operator can start from. Three commits: `6c98c0fa` (the executable-shape fixes and their nine pins), `38eb8716` (item 36's own record plus the Brev 4B pins), `aea3a40a` (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-28-a5500-operator-handoff.md`).

    - **The handoff is hand-written on purpose, and says so.** The A5500 checklist is generated and stays the authority on what each row means; the handoff deliberately does not restate the four weight sha256s or the S1 arithmetic — it points at the sections that carry them, because a second copy of a number is a second way to be wrong. What it adds is the parts no spec-row layout produces: fetch order (weights BEFORE the precheck, since nothing in the repo can tell you they are missing), the `--env-file .env` distinction, the build-because-nothing-to-pull step, the two-phase `--no-deps` up, the measurement order (8B → probes at N≥3 → S1 with the real resident set → S4 with both cold starts → the 4B fallback row → M1's chain), a paste-back contract (verbatim numbers, blocked rows with their output, aggregates only per D10), and the stop list — bars change by owner ruling, the M2 pick reopens by its flip conditions and not by an operator, and the two recorded gaps get worked around, not fixed mid-run.

    - **Two hazards found while verifying my own new document — both mine, both the class this ledger already warns about.** (a) **A stale anchor in a document about not having stale anchors:** the handoff first pinned head `38eb8716`, which is its own parent — the file did not exist there, so an operator who obeyed the pin exactly would have arrived and not found the document they were sent for. Found by going to check my own claim; the pin is now `aea3a40a` (the commit that adds the file) with the reason spelled out in the text. (b) **An invalid formatter test, caught by the hook rather than by me.** I tested whether prettier would corrupt the handoff's mid-word underscores by running it on a **`/tmp` copy** — which resolves config from the file's own location, so it used prettier's defaults instead of the repo's and reported "unchanged." The real hook, run from the repo root, modified the file in ten places. The damage is worse than the underscore class item 36 records: prettier also **dedents a wrapped inline-code continuation out of its bullet**, which mangles a command line an operator copies verbatim. Fix: the handoff joins the two checklists in `.prettierignore`, and the durable rule is that a formatter test must run inside the tree, never on a copy. Also re-verified the same way: the four digests now have a pin requiring all FOUR (not just the two weights — the `mmproj` is where the F16 trap lives) with a `len(…) == 4` guard so the membership test cannot pass vacuously, and detect-secrets was satisfied only once the digest sat at a nesting depth where the pragma fits inside 100 columns, because ruff-format's wrap orphans a trailing pragma and the scanner then rejects it (both forms checked against the real hook).

    - **Gates, from their own output.** Precheck suite **57 passed** (re-run after the pin restructure, not assumed). Full unit tier on the pushed head: `1 failed, 30586 passed, 123 skipped, 8 xfailed` with the only `FAILED` line being the sanctioned `test_skips_when_skip_build` — and a dated correction to my own report: I quoted "30662 passed, 53 skipped" once in the same turn **from memory before reading the log**; the log line above is the figure, and the misquote is the "declare only from the command's own output" rule caught in the act, in a session whose whole subject is claims that outran their evidence. Two facts the handoff asserts were checked at the file rather than inherited: `build_gen2` has **no CLI** (no argparse/`__main__` in `eval_store.py` — it is a function, so the handoff says "the shipped `build_gen2(…)` driver" and never invents a `--generation` flag, which is exactly the invented-script failure item 29 records), and `data/synthetic` carries **413** label sets. Also newly measured for the record: gating the hooks with `--files` **rewrites `.secrets.baseline` scoped to those files** — 138,744 lines deleted in the working tree, restored and never staged — so any future run that gates a subset must expect and undo that.

    **CLOSE POINTER: [V] as an artifact; it closes no milestone.** It is what makes the A5500 run startable by someone other than me. Still owner-owned: the run itself (which closes M1's assertions 2/3 and produces S1/S4 — item 35's close), whether `ai-llm` is ever profile-gated in prod compose, `S2_MAX`/`S3_MIN`'s `[?]` rows, Brev spend, and every PR/merge. The media agent's synthetic corpus is the input that finally makes S2/S3 answerable, and this handoff tells the operator to record its generation beside their numbers rather than assume one.

38. **A5500 BOX PREP ON THE REAL HARDWARE: weights, images and one fix the run needed, and a re-ID export that passes its own validation with a mostly random backbone (2026-09-28) [V].** First slice executed on the A5500 itself, driven from item 37's handoff at head `6fa7942e`. The box is not the cold, single-card machine the handoff was written for, and the differences are recorded here so the S1/S4 rows that follow can be read against them.

    - **The machine, as measured.** `nvidia-smi`: `NVIDIA RTX A5500, 8.6, 24564 MiB` at index 0 **plus an `NVIDIA RTX A400, 8.6, 4094 MiB` at index 1**, driver `610.43.03`; `nvidia-ctk cdi list` exposes both (`nvidia.com/gpu=0`, `=1`, `=all`). D2's "the A400 is retired" is a deployment fact, not a physical one: the card is installed and in the CDI set. Every `GPU_*` is now 0, so nothing is placed on it, but **the S1 sampler must be `nvidia-smi -i 0 --query-gpu=memory.used --format=csv -l 1`**, or each sample prints both cards. The A5500 also carries the desktop compositor (`kwin_wayland`, 95 MiB used at idle), and that baseline is part of every S1 reading on this box. Fedora 44, SELinux enforcing, rootless podman, `docker compose` 5.3.1 provider.
    - **Env: patched, not regenerated.** The box already had a generated `.env` (2026-09-14, real credentials matching existing volumes), so the template's deltas were applied onto it rather than re-running setup.py. Applied: all `GPU_*`=0, `CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=86`, the rev 7 `VLM_*` identity, `AI_MODELS_PATH=/export/models`, and `BACKEND_MODEL_PRELOAD=true` (see the fix below). **Not applied: `TMPDIR`.** Nothing in `docker-compose.prod.yml` reads it, this `.env` never set it, and adding it arms the four-test `write_runtime_env` trap the precheck itself warns about. `a5500_precheck.py --env-file .env` then printed **no FAILs** (WARNs: the A400 in the CDI set, ai-llm unprofiled, no ghcr ai-vlm, stale pycache that was cleared before gating).
    - **Weights: all eleven files, each checked against a repo pin.** `AI_MODELS_PATH=/export/models` is a root-owned symlink to `/home/msvoboda/models` (the only large filesystem). `vlm/`: all four GGUFs, `sha256sum -c` OK against the checklist's four pins, byte counts exact, and **mode 644 on arrival** (`hf download` into a local dir; finding F did not reproduce this way, but it stays a check, not an assumption). `model-zoo/`: `yolo26m.pt` OK against `ai/download_models.sh`'s pin; OSNet MSMT17 OK against `models.yml` (stored under the long name with a **relative** short-name symlink, which the loader's path guard requires); the face pair OK against `models.yml`'s two pins; the tiktoken `cl100k_base` cache OK against tiktoken's own `expected_hash`. **The face pair's source is not in the repo:** `insightface` release `v0.7` `buffalo_l.zip` (`det_10g.onnx` renamed, `w600k_r50.onnx`). The pins are what make that URL trustworthy, and both matched. **`scripts/download-model-zoo.py` cannot be used for this:** it writes to a hardcoded `/export/ai_models` and fetches ImageNet OSNet weights under the MSMT17 short name, which would fail the pin. The fetch used the sources `ai/download_models.sh` and `setup_lib/model_downloader.py` already name.
    - **Images: built here, and the driver trap checked, not assumed.** `ai-vlm:sm86` digest `sha256:5f5c2d71dbdb0ed6da3e302c7e0441e7594d40bee4dbe15490d7be8461b71a41`, llama-server `version: 7972 (e06088da0)`, and `--list-devices` inside the container: `CUDA0: NVIDIA RTX A5500 (24102 MiB, 23749 MiB free)`, `compute capability 8.6`. That is the compute_cap/build pair on one line; `/props` build_info follows at serve time. `libcuda.so.1` resolves to the host's `/usr/lib64` (CDI). The image's `cuda-13.3/compat` (610.43.02) is not on the loader path, so the forward-compat reject that silently dropped the old ai-llm image to CPU on this same 610 host does **not** fire for ai-vlm. `ai-gateway`, `backend` and `frontend` were rebuilt `--no-cache`: the prior images were 7 months and 2 weeks old and predated rev 6 residency and the 1.6 frontend.
    - **FIX (this commit): the prod container could never load the specialists.** (a) `Settings.backend_model_preload` gates main.py's boot sweep and defaults False. setup.py writes `BACKEND_MODEL_PRELOAD` into `.env`, but the backend has no `env_file:` and no compose file passed it, so on every compose box the face and re-ID legs answered "unavailable" with both hash-pinned weights on disk. Item 32 fixed the sweep and the inclusive 24 GB threshold, not the wire into the container. (b) The prod backend image had no onnxruntime at all: it is in no base dependency (walked from `uv.lock`), only the `face`/`alpr` extras, and the builder's `uv sync` took no `--extra`. The fix is `BACKEND_MODEL_PRELOAD=${BACKEND_MODEL_PRELOAD:-false}` on the backend (default = the Settings default, so a box that never set it boots as before), the var declared in `.env.example`, and `--extra face` on the builder sync (onnxruntime 1.30.0 ships cp314 manylinux_2_28 wheels for x86_64 and aarch64, so the GB300 build is unaffected). Four pins went **red first for the right reason** (`TestSpecialistResidencyReachesTheBackend` x3, `test_prod_dependencies_carry_the_face_extra`), then green. **Runtime proof in the rebuilt image:** onnxruntime `1.30.0` imports; the face detector and recognizer load through `face_recognizer_loader` (CPUExecutionProvider, 512-d embedding, L2 norm 1.0000); OSNet loads through `osnet_loader` with 2 missing keys (the classifier only). Owner ruling 2026-09-28: fix both, then measure.
    - **A correction to my own first read, before it reached a number:** the backend image's torch is `2.14.0+cpu`, so the backend's OSNet is a CPU resident. The preload fix is M1's specialist half, **not** an S1 term. S1's specialist VRAM on this box is the gateway's `yolo26` + `reid` only.
    - **FINDING, not fixed: the Triton `reid` export is a mostly random network that passes its own validation.** `ai/gateway/export/export_reid.py` hand-writes **plain OSNet** (`conv2a..conv2d` blocks, a BatchNorm stem plus a separate `conv1_IN`), while the MSMT17 checkpoint is **OSNet-AIN** (`conv2.N.layers.*` streams, an instance-norm stem, separate `pool2`/`pool3`). Keys matched: **118 of the model's 577** (classifier, fc, conv5 and some 1x1 layers); the other 459 stay at random init. It passes because `load_state_dict(strict=False)` only warns, unexpected keys are logged at DEBUG, and the ONNX is "validated" against the same under-loaded PyTorch model (cosine 1.000 at batch 1/2/4/8). It is the same self-referential-check pattern as item 35(a). **Blast radius, as traced:** the vlm verdict path's `person_reid` leg uses the backend's torchreid `osnet_loader` (above, correct), and no `vlm_*` module references `enrichment_client`, the Triton `reid` model's only caller (legacy enrichment). The broken file changes no vlm verdict. It stays resident because the vlm residency set names it, and its graph and weight sizes are identical to a correct export, so **S1 is unaffected**. It was exported to `triton/reid/1/` for exactly that footprint and must not be read as a working re-ID model. Fixing it is a re-ID change and outside this run's scope (item 37); left to the owner.
    - **Also noted, not touched:** the plate leg's `fast-alpr` (`alpr` extra) is still absent from the prod image; `docker-compose.yml`/`ghcr.yml` do not pass `BACKEND_MODEL_PRELOAD` (ghcr cannot serve vlm mode at all).
    - **Gates (executed, this tree):** `backend/tests/unit/core` plus every unit test reading `.env.example`, prod compose or `backend/Dockerfile`, plus the precheck suite: **4129 passed, 0 failed**. Stale `__pycache__` was cleared before the run (the precheck's deletion-guard trap).

    **CLOSE POINTER: [V] as prep; it closes no milestone.** S1, S4, the P0.3/tool-call probes and M1's chain are the next rows and are not claimed here. Owner-owned: the `export_reid.py` fix (and whether to guard `strict=False` exports generally), the `alpr` extra, and whether the other compose files get the preload wire.

39. **A5500 RUN, 8B PRIMARY: S1 and S4 read PASS on this corpus, the probes hold at N>=3 on two server builds, and M1's chain ran end to end - after 16 fixes the chain itself surfaced (2026-09-28) [V].** Box, env and weights are item 38. The serving image is `ai-vlm:sm86` (llama.cpp `b7972-e06088da0`), rebuilt once in this row so that the KV cache fix below applies; digests `sha256:5f5c2d71…` (f16 KV) and then `sha256:d165a409…` (q8_0 KV). Every reading below is from the q8_0 image unless it says otherwise.

    - **Identity, on one line as the handout asks:** `compute_cap=8.6`, `/props build_info=b7972-e06088da0`, model `Qwen3VL-8B-Instruct-Q4_K_M` + `mmproj-…-Q8_0`, `modalities.vision=True`, `CTX_SIZE=32768 PARALLEL=2` (16384/slot), `offloaded 37/37 layers to GPU` (no CPU offload; the 333.84 MiB `CPU_Mapped` buffer is llama.cpp's token-embedding host copy, not layers). `projected to use 7216 MiB`. KV line: `llama_kv_cache: size = 2448.00 MiB ( 16384 cells, 36 layers, 2/2 seqs), K (q8_0): 1224.00 MiB, V (q8_0): 1224.00 MiB`.
    - **The KV disagreement (items 35/36) is settled by that line and its predecessor.** The first serve logged `size = 4608.00 MiB … K (f16), V (f16)`: 4608 MiB is the WHOLE 2x16384 pool at f16 (~144 KB/token), which is what the bake-off recorded, and the older "~73 KB/token, ~2.4 GB" prose was the q8_0 figure all along. It was f16 because `ai/vlm/Dockerfile` never passed `CACHE_TYPE_K/V` to llama-server although compose set q8_0 - fixed in `58163f23f` (with a pin that fails if compose ever sets an ai-vlm var the image never reads). Resident set at idle went 11604 -> 9376 MiB.
    - **S1 [V, PASS on this corpus]: peak 9606 MiB (9.38 GiB) vs the 20,890 MiB bar, headroom ~11.3 GB.** Sampler `nvidia-smi -i 0 --query-gpu=timestamp,memory.used -lms 250` (703 samples) across a full-store replay plus two concurrent heaviest-batch replays (both slots busy), resident set `ai-gateway` (yolo26 + reid, tritonserver 676 MiB) + `ai-vlm` (llama-server peak 8822 MiB) + `backend` (0 MiB: its torch is `+cpu`, item 38) + the desktop compositor 17-21 MiB. Caveat, stated not smoothed: the heaviest batch this corpus can produce is **7443 tokens** (`prowling`, 4 images; `n_tokens` from llama-server's own log), not the ~12.2K worst case - the corpus's media items carry no detection rows. The KV pool is allocated at load, so a longer prompt fills it rather than growing it; the unmeasured part is compute-buffer growth.
    - **S4 [V, PASS on this corpus]: 21 measured requests, nearest-rank p95 21.7 s, max 22.6 s, bar 30 s.** Warm sequential (13 items): median 7592 ms, max 9247 ms (item 1 includes the one-time client probe, 4419 ms). Both slots busy on the heaviest batch: 17.5-22.6 s per item including a per-process probe (4.7-14.5 s under contention; in the product the probe runs once per backend process). **Cold restart:** page cache dropped (`Cached` 3.68 GB after, below the pair's 5.78 GB), `podman restart` -> `/health` 200 in 12.5 s against the 120 s start_period (10 s of that is llama-server ignoring SIGTERM until SIGKILL - a small finding), first request 12.8 s. **Wake:** after 300 s idle `is_sleeping=true` and llama-server fell 8822 -> 290 MiB (GPU0 1074 MiB); first request 13.1 s, its probe 6254 ms vs 4.4-4.7 s warm, so the wake itself is ~1.5-2 s against the client's 90 s timeout. Replay report `run_id e15163de5da3457aa34b047c8807c52a`: S5 `unparseable 0, refusals 0, unavailable 0` of 13; verdict mix confirmed 10 / uncertain 3.
    - **P0.3 probes, N>=3 on both server builds:** `enforcement.py` 5/5 ENFORCED ("nonce const echoed") on the f16 image and again 5/5 on the q8_0 image; `tool_calls.py --image` 3/3 SUPPORTED with `required_arg_honoured: true` on each. Flip condition (ii)'s tool-calling half is answered for the 8B on this box.

S3 is score-vs-expected-floor, which the box fix does not address; the bar is the owner's.

    - **FIX `2fd5c54bc`: boxes in Qwen3-VL's own grounding format.** The Qwen3-VL cookbook's `bbox_2d` is `[x1, y1, x2, y2]` on a 0-1000 scale of the frame; read that way, event 617's `[369, 183, 419, 513]` is a sliver, which is exactly what the model said. The rendered rows now carry `bbox_2d` scaled by the row's linked frame (or the one size every attached frame shares), clamped; a row with no readable frame size keeps pixels and the pixel sentence. Snapshot rows keep `[x, y, w, h]`. Owner ruling 2026-09-28.

The item 39 replay passed only because its replies were shorter. The longest 8B verdict, by llama-server's own eval count, is **852 tokens**; the budget is 1024 (1.2x, pinned against 852) and the probe budget follows at +64. Owner ruling 2026-09-28.
    - **FIX `d7b3fd3c6`: the prompt fit did not match the served slot.** Taking S1 at a genuinely full slot, the client's own fit sent a batch llama-server refused: `request (22293 tokens) exceeds the available context size (16384 tokens)`. (a) Images: the fit reserves 1280 tokens per still, but uncapped Qwen3-VL spends ~1 token per 32x32 px - four 1.9-2.4 MP stills were **8,509** prompt tokens (`usage`), not 5,120. `ai-vlm` now sets `LLAMA_ARG_IMAGE_MAX_TOKENS=1280` (read by llama-server; `--help` at b7972), pinned to the client constant; the same four stills are now **5,003**. Env-wired, so the measured image is not rebuilt. (b) Text: the same fitted prompt was 10,237 tokens by the repo's counter and **13,785** by the engine's `/tokenize` (1.35x; Qwen splits every digit and rows are mostly digits). The fit now budgets 1.5 served tokens per counted token (pinned at >= 1.1x the measured gap), deterministically, so the stored `llm_prompt` is still the prompt sent. (c) The refusal was a `VlmTransportError`: a breaker failure, then a §6 retry of the same bytes for a second - five such batches open `ai-vlm` for every camera. It is now `VlmContextOverflowError`, raised once, counted as `vlm_context_overflow`, never fed to the breaker, still `verification_failed`. Owner ruling 2026-09-28. Also `9129e1f32`: the contract round-trip leg had been red since `881a6e9d0` (it compared the model's provenance with the client's stamp) - I had run only the unit suites then; it now pins the stamp.

Both slots at once, four 1.9-2.4 MP stills, resident set as item 39: **8B uncapped (pre-`d7b3fd3c6`), 14,936-token prompts: peak 9,826 MiB (9.60 GiB)**; **8B with the landed fit and cap, 400 rows trimmed by the client to 14,130-token prompts, 6/6 served, 0 overflows: peak 9,722 MiB (9.49 GiB)**. Bar 20.4 GiB; offload none (37/37). The vision encoder's buffer grows with image size, so the uncapped reading is the conservative one.
    - **S4 at the ceiling.** Pre-cap, two concurrent full slots ran attempts past the 25 s `AI_VLM_READ_TIMEOUT`: a transport error, then a faster retry on the server's cached prompt, 35-47 s end to end. With the cap: per-attempt **6.6-22.3 s** excluding the per-process probe (12.2-30.5 s including it; the product probes once per backend process), no retries. Item 39's corpus S4 (p95 21.7 s) stands; the ceiling is recorded, not part of that p95.

A warm-cache recreate of the 8B: 1.5 s. Item 39's 12.5 s restart was ~10 s of llama-server ignoring SIGTERM plus the same ~2.5 s load.

Probes: enforcement 5/5 ENFORCED, tool_calls 3/3 SUPPORTED (`required_arg_honoured: true`). **S1: peak 7,192 MiB (7.02 GiB)** on item 39's procedure; **7,228 MiB (7.06 GiB)** at the capped ceiling. Load 2.6 s. **But the 4B fails S5 on this box:** the 13-item gen-2 replay (run `35a4b5ec`) returned **8 of 13** `VlmTruncatedError` at `max_tokens=1024` (the 8B: 0 of 13, run `e15163de`), and at the ceiling 4 of 6 verdicts truncated and one in-client probe hit its 1088 budget before the const (fails closed as `ConstrainedDecodingNotEnforced`). The 4B's five complete replies were 1,046-1,705 characters (~300-500 tokens) while the failures ran past 1,024 tokens - a split that reads as runaway generation rather than a wordier model, but the truncated bodies are not stored, so that is a hypothesis, not a measurement. **For flip condition (i): if the 8B ever failed S1, the fallback has a fitting S1 number, but not a passing S5.**

PermissionError(13)" then "Continuing without orchestrator" on every start today; the usual fix (`label=disable` on the backend) trades away confinement, so it is a security call. Item 39's orchestrator change was therefore never exercised live on this box. (b) The backend cannot write `/app/data/logs/*.log` (EACCES with no AVC - ownership, not SELinux). (c) An empty untracked `backend/api/helpers/` (a deleted module's leftover) reddened a contract pin locally; removed.

42. **R8 SLICE S0 — CURRENCY, SHIPPED ALONE: three stale status lines corrected, a gate that keeps them corrected, and R8's landing docs (2026-09-29) [V].** PR [#6714](https://github.com/mikesvoboda/nemotron-v3-home-security-intelligence/pull/6714), commit `6798ee59`, branched off `main` at `51f635e5`. No code changes.

    - **The defect.** `docs/vss-integration/README.md:6`, `AGENTS.md:6` and `00-context.md:5` (their **pre-fix** positions; the inserted banners pushed each stale line down) all asserted nothing was implemented, six days after `4bfd6fa4` (2026-09-27, #6681) made the VLM path the shipped default at `docker-compose.prod.yml:578`. An agent routing from `AGENTS.md` - the file titled "Start here" - would have concluded the effort was un-started. No CI could see it: `agents-md.yml` validates presence and references, not content, and `scripts/docs-drift-rules.yml` carries no vss rule. The gate matches on **phrases, not line numbers**, precisely so the banner insertion cannot make it drift. **[V]**
    - **The form.** Per the in-doc precedent at `06-repo-a-readiness.md:11`: the false sentence **stays** (frozen research prose), with a dated blockquote **above** it. Order is the rule - a correction the reader meets after the lie corrects nothing. Each banner names the sentence it retires.
    - **`scripts/check-vss-docs-currency.py` + `backend/tests/unit/scripts/test_check_vss_docs_currency.py`, red-first.** `uv run pytest ... -p no:randomly -n0` -> **10 passed**; the 10 ERRORs are at **teardown** only (`ImportError: libxcb.so.1` via the conftest's `cv2`, the standing sandbox gap, item 40's class). `python3 scripts/check-vss-docs-currency.py` -> **ok, exit 0**. `ruff check` / `ruff format --check` / `mypy` clean. `agents_md_validator.py` -> **dead_links: 0**, exit 0. **[V]**
    - **Two defects the gate itself shipped with, caught before commit and now pinned by tests.** Both are _plausible prose a future author will write_, which is what makes them the finding rather than the typo-hunting:
      1. It searched the claim's **first** occurrence and asked "is a banner above it?" A correction that **quotes the sentence it retires** (`The line below ("Nothing is implemented yet.") is stale`) makes that first occurrence the banner's own line - so a correctly written doc FAILED. Fixed: occurrences are sought **outside** banner blocks, and a claim standing only inside a banner is reported as the frozen-prose violation it is.
      2. It accepted **any** dated banner above the line. `00-context.md:3` carries an Errata banner about _other_ claims and satisfied the gate while its status line stood uncorrected - a **false pass**, the worse failure. Fixed: a banner counts only if it **names** the claim, matched on the joined block (these docs are wrapped prose; a quoted sentence breaks mid-phrase). And `Status` is **not** an accepted lead-in, because the stale `README.md` line is itself a dated `> **Status (2026-09-23): ...**` blockquote - it would vouch for itself.
         **Mutation-checked:** deleting one banner from a copy of the real tree makes the gate FAIL naming `AGENTS.md`. A gate that only ever passes is not a gate. **[V]**
    - **My own banners carried the defect class they were fixing, and both were caught pre-commit.** I cited `ledger :403` for S3's failure - `:403` is the p95 sentence; the anchors are **`:401`** (landed arm `10/20`) and **`:221`** (F14, `S3_MIN = 90%`). And I wrote "M1-M4 are still open" when the ledger supports only **M1** (close pointer `:415`: "the notification link is unwired") and **M2** (the VLM pick is owner-owned, `12` R10); M3/M4 were my inference, not a reading. Corrected to exactly what the sources say. The lesson generalises: **re-verify an anchor the turn before you cite it**, including one you wrote yourself earlier the same day. **[V]**
    - **Coverage direction: unchanged, by construction.** `coverage.run.source = ["backend"]` (`pyproject.toml:612`) cannot see `scripts/`, and the new test lives under the omitted `backend/tests/*` (`:614`). Neither merged floor (84 unit / 37 integration) moves, and no floor was touched. **[V]**
    - **R8's landing docs, per `12-postponed-roadmap.md:10`.** Its own spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-r8-legacy-retirement-design.md` (What-goes / What-stays / six slices / five open owner items), the measured scope `docs/plans/2026-09-28-r8-legacy-retirement-scope.md`, the ordered goal prompt, and R8 marked in the index at `:23` plus a banner at §R8 - both recording that the owner **reversed** the "go-live holds 14 days" trigger rather than meeting it, with the two rulings verbatim. **[V]**
    - **Formatting trap worth carrying.** The repo's `package.json` wants prettier `^3.7.4` and the frontend lock resolves **3.9.9**, but the pre-commit hook's real version is its `additional_dependencies: prettier@3.2.4` (`rev: v3.1.0` is only the hook-repo tag). Running 3.9.9 re-padded **all 14** unrelated rows of the doc-12 index because one widened cell changes the whole table's alignment; the 3.9.9-clean state is not the 3.2.4-clean state. Reverted and reformatted under **3.2.4** - net diff on that table is **one row**. `docs/vss-integration/*.md` is not in `.prettierignore`, so the dunder-corruption class does not exempt it: the mitigation is backticking every `__identifier__`, which held here. **[V]**
    - **Sandbox note.** `git commit` fails here with "Author identity unknown" - no `user.*` is configured and `gh auth status` shows logged-out (expected, CLAUDE.md). Commits are made per-invocation with `-c user.name/-c user.email` matching the history's dominant identity; the mounted repo's `.git/config` is deliberately left unmodified. **[V]**

43. **R8 SLICE S1 - THE HARD RAISE AND THE BRANCH DELETION, SHIPPED: `PIPELINE_MODE=legacy` now raises at boot and every branch site the wide grep enumerated is gone (2026-09-29) [V].** PR [#6716](https://github.com/mikesvoboda/nemotron-v3-home-security-intelligence/pull/6716), commit `c98d36af`, branched off `main` at `51f635e5`. **Read this row's number with care:** the row was written, and prettier 3.2.4-clean, as `42.` on a base where the S0 row (#6714) had not yet landed - the formatter renumbers every marker in an ordered list to the sequential next value, so it silently RENUMBERS an appended row, not just realigns it (item 42's row carried the same formatter's trap for table cells). #6714 landed first, so this row lands as `43.`; the slice is identified by its heading, PR and commit, never the numeral. Owner rulings verbatim (spec §0): **"we do not have to support backwards compatability"** and **"we should hard raise"**.

    - **The defect the raise closes.** The validator at `backend/core/config.py` ACCEPTED `"legacy"` and logged a warning; a stale `PIPELINE_MODE=legacy` in a deployment's `.env` booted the unsupported pipeline and looked like a normal boot. A warning is not a guard. It now raises for `legacy` and every unknown value (the never-silently-fallback rule, spec §2:81-84), and an UNSET mode still defaults to `vlm` - pinned so the raise cannot widen into "missing `PIPELINE_MODE` crashes every deployment" (the prod compose default is `${PIPELINE_MODE:-vlm}`). **[V]**
    - **The raise is written branch-free on purpose, and that is the design, not an omission.** The validator never writes `if value == "legacy"` - it accepts `"vlm"` or raises, with the retired spelling named only in the message text. That shape is forced by the new guard `backend/tests/unit/test_no_legacy_pipeline_branches.py`: an AST scan failing any shipped `backend/*.py` that COMPARES anything against the `"legacy"` string constant. A validator that special-cased the word would be the one branch the gate exists to prevent. Independent scan at this head: legacy comparisons in shipped code: **none**. **[V]**
    - **The deletions** (the WIDE grep's 13 sites - the narrow grep misses locals that re-derive the mode): mode branches in `main.py` (the AI-service health monitor list is built by `build_ai_service_health_configs`, YOLO26 only - on 2026-09-28 the retired pair it used to carry made the monitor probe `nemotron`, find it down, and attempt to restart the 30B `ai-llm` onto the same 24 GB GPU `ai-vlm` needs; the restart failed only because the podman socket was unreachable), `api/routes/health_ai_services.py`, `api/routes/system.py`, `core/container.py`, `services/container_orchestrator.py` (the retired LLM containers are never registered), `services/pipeline_factory.py`, `services/system_broadcaster.py`. Mode-fiction residue renamed: `ai_services_config_for_mode` -> `shipped_ai_services_config` (the `settings` param was unread - ARG001 - and `_for_mode` described a branch that no longer exists; noqa would have kept the confusion R8 exists to remove), `_check_vlm_mode_ai_services_health` -> `_check_shipped_ai_services_health`. **[V]**

Integration `legacy_wire` narrowed to wire-only (its mode pin hard-raises); the fast-path e2e test kept its coverage via an injected recording analyzer and now pins that the shipped fast path routes through the batch gate and never writes nemotron's `is_fast_path` column. **[V]**

S1 sweep (the 15 touched files incl. deploy tests): **713 passed**. Full backend unit suite: **32,495 passed, 64 failed + 1 error** - and the failures were PROVEN pre-existing, not assumed: the identical sweep run on `HEAD` from a `git stash push --include-untracked` tree fails the same environmental set (cv2/libxcb-class files S1 never touches, the `systemctl`-missing deploy phase - item 40's class), and the strict set-diff against the union of two HEAD runs is **empty in both directions**. 44. **R8 SLICE S2b - THE DELETE LANDS: the legacy LLM analyzer and the whole enrichment tier are gone, the survivors are re-homed on `ai-vlm`, and the test surface is green behind them (2026-09-29) [V].** Commits [`602379e2`](https://github.com/mikesvoboda/nemotron-v3-home-security-intelligence/commit/602379e2) (the deletion + re-home) and [`6266c96d`](https://github.com/mikesvoboda/nemotron-v3-home-security-intelligence/commit/6266c96d) (the test-side fallout, census adjudication). PR stacked on S1's branch. S2a ([`0ba90d5f`](https://github.com/mikesvoboda/nemotron-v3-home-security-intelligence/commit/0ba90d5f)) did the EXTRACT first - the constrained-decoding vocabulary already lived in `backend/services/constrained_decoding.py` with its four shipped importers repointed, which is what made the delete possible without touching the VLM path.

Spec §2's three survivors stay: `face_recognizer_loader`, `osnet_loader`, `fast_alpr_loader` (+ `model_loader_base`) - face/plate lookups are DB lookups a VLM cannot do from pixels. `ai/` dirs were removed with `git rm`, never `rm -rf`.

    - **Two deliberate keeps, so a future reader does not "fix" them.** (1) The DI registry still registers `VLMAnalyzer` under the STRING `"nemotron_analyzer"`: `pipeline_factory` is the only class-choice seam, `main.py:763` + `core/dependencies.py:95` resolve by that name, and `backend/tests/unit/test_r8_s2b_nemotron_deletion.py::TestDiNameIsDeliberate` pins it as a decision. **The rename is S3's, ruled in-session 2026-09-29** - the retirement of a class and the renaming of a wiring string are different risks and one slice carries one of them. (2) `RETIRED_LLM_SERVICES = {"ai-llm","ai-llm-vllm"}` stays in `container_orchestrator.py`: discovery lists stopped containers, and the guard must keep refusing to manage a stale pre-R8 container on a host that still has one. `ALLOWED_RESTART_SCRIPTS` shrank to `{"ai/start_detector.sh"}` - a restart allowlist must not stay pointed at a retired engine.
    - **P0.3's startup gate did not lose its teeth.** S2b deleted `test_p03_constrained_verdict.py` (1,515 L) and `test_p03_verification_producer.py` with the analyzer they were built on; `TestStartupGateSurvives` in the S2b guard file now pins `run_constrained_startup_check` behaviorally (probe-pass → `enforced` **crossing the registry by the retired name**, `IGNORED` → `not_enforced` not a crash, disabled → `disabled` without network, endpoint-down → `inconclusive`, and the gate ordering BEFORE `main.py`'s swallowing Redis block). The verdict vocabulary keeps exactly one home: `constrained_decoding.py`, pinned by `test_one_home_for_the_vocabulary`.
    - **Command + result (all re-run on the committed tree, not inherited).** Contracts: `PYTHONPATH=/tmp/cv2stub uv run pytest -q -p no:randomly backend/tests/contracts` → **646 passed, 0 failed** (at the deletion's edge it was 8 failed / 638 passed). Unit tier: same invocation on `backend/tests/unit` → **27,294 passed, 124 skipped, 8 xfailed, 35 failed + 1 error**, and the 35+1 are all the pre-existing `/tmp/cv2stub` `InvalidSpecError: Cannot autospec attr 'VideoCapture'` class in `test_frame_extractor` / `test_rtsp_test_service` / `test_stream_manager` - files untouched since `53c93d20`; this goal prompt's own MINES says "66/96 error at import here (no cv2) - judge by CI". `uv run mypy backend/ synthbench/` → **Success, 1614 files**. `ruff check backend/ synthbench/` + `ruff format --check` → clean. `scripts/check-test-collection.py backend frontend ai` → **4108 files, all collect >= 1**. `check-flake-allowlist.py` → 0 entries. `check-ai-provider-parity.py --expect .github/ai-parity-baseline.json` → exit 0. `gen-ai-contract.py --check` → current. `ratchet-check.py` → **exit 0** (was 85 violations here); `suppression-census.py --expect "$(cat .github/suppression-baseline.json)"` → exit 0; `scripts/test_suppression_census.py` + `test_ratchet_check.py` → **29 passed**. `prettier --check` on every touched md/yaml/json → all match (3.9.9 reproduces the committed shape; the 3.2.4 ordered-list renumbering trap is why the row's own number is stated rather than trusted).

The distinction that mattered: 68 STALE entries licensed mock-patches in files this slice deleted, so they die with their sites ("entries must not outlive what they license") - but the 16 LAUNDER/UNREGISTERED sites in `conftest.py` and `e2e/test_gpu_pipeline.py` were **not new suppressions**: they are the SAME 11 skips line-SHIFTED by this commit's deletions, because the imperative-skip ids are `file:lineno`. Their written verdicts were carried forward by re-keying `HOST_JUSTIFIED` (6 keys) and the registry rows (11), and the two batch25 adjudications died with their file. One pre-existing orphan surfaced here and was fixed where found: S1 renamed `test_check_ai_services_health_nemotron_down` → `..._ai_vlm_down` at `734f5e40` and left the registry entry behind (S1's `ratchet-check` was already exit 1 with those 2 violations - measured in a clean `github/main` worktree, where main itself is exit 0). **No floor was lowered (84 unit / 37 integration, `ci.yml:613`/`:1215`), no quarantine widened, nothing hand-edited.**
    - **Characterization doctrine, the part a junior will want.** A pin that reads a deleted file's text has three honest answers and the file says which it got: RETARGET to the surviving shipped text (`g4` → `florence_client.py:94`, the fourth spelling's surviving home, still exactly one literal; `g5` → `reid_service.py:523` + a tombstone assert that the deleted file stays deleted; `N1b` → the B5 fixture pair's surviving home `test_household.py:738/742`, which is what its own escape hatch instructs); become a TOMBSTONE when the defect was deleted rather than fixed and there is no surviving text (`V3`'s enrichment `type`/`class_name` precedence split was LOCAL to `enrichment_pipeline.py` - the test now states the hazard in full and asserts the file's absence; the provider-wire-key half of V3 still pins live in the same class); or read a FROZEN VERBATIM COPY when the pin is an adjudicated evidence record that must not go silent (`TestS5ThinkTagStripping`, the D1-D6 Tier-A record). The last one is new and is worth the rule it sets: `backend/tests/contracts/ai_providers/retired_providers.py` is evidence-only, imported by nothing that ships, never collected, and its code was extracted BY LINE RANGE from `git show 0ba90d5f:...` rather than retyped, so the regex literals are byte-identical to what made the claims true. Deleting the pins would have made the contract tier silently quiet about a defect set the parity golden list still adjudicates; a skip would have been a suppression needing a registry entry AND would assert nothing; `git show` at test time is un-runnable on CI's shallow checkouts.

S4: the dated DROP SQL for the tier's tables. S5: the frontend panels (`EnrichmentViewer`/`Badges`/`Panel`, `EventEnrichmentSummary`, `poseVisualization.ts`) and the docs sweep - which now owns **`README.md:47` and `:125`** (still narrate Nemotron-3-Nano + the `ai-llm` container as "the brain of this system") and the two docs that still print the deleted `nemotron_api_key` (`docs/architecture/observability/structured-logging.md:214`, `docs/architecture/security/network-security.md:379`); the root `AGENTS.md` engine lines were retired in THIS slice because `602379e2` had made its own `ai/` tree row false (`ai/nemotron/` listed, `ai/vlm/` missing) - fixing a fact your own commit broke is not the doc sweep, rewriting narrative prose is, so no model name was asserted anywhere: the ledger's D5 ruling keeps model identity a config choice. Kept-and-DEAD, flagged not deleted (deletion is a slice that must say so): `scene_ocr_service.py` (its caller tier is gone), `trajectory_analyzer.py` (orphaned the same way), `ai_fallback.py`'s zero-consumer binding, `QUANTIZATION_RECOMMENDATIONS`' florence/nemotron rows, and `setup_lib/model_downloader`'s deprecated lists and now-unreachable downloaders. API-surface discipline held: `NemotronMetrics` / `PerformanceUpdate.nemotron` / `collect_nemotron_metrics` keep their names because the frontend consumes them - renaming a public shape is not part of deleting an engine. `notification_filter.py:35 should_notify` untouched, per the hard rule.

Direction as understood and to be confirmed by the CI Coverage Gate on this PR: the shipped surface shrank, the surviving VLM path keeps its pins (646 contracts, 67 guard/hoist/no-legacy tests), and both floors stay exactly where they were. `Test Coverage Gate` on S1's PR is currently red for an UNRELATED reason - a pytest `INTERNALERROR` from a `xdist` worker assertion in `test_calibration.py::TestUserCalibrationCreate::test_create_threshold_below_zero_invalid`, with the run otherwise reporting 17,741 passed - i.e. a flake in the gate's own pytest invocation, not a coverage shortfall; it is being watched, not worked around.

It retires no provider rows (S3), no tables (S4), no frontend panels or doc narrative (S5).
